v+d[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}\not\models\mathit{Inv}^{A^{1}\wedge A^{2}}(l_{3})
. In this case, we know that
v+d[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}\not\models\mathit{Inv}^{A^{1}\wedge A^{2}}(l_{3})
implies that
v+d[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}\not\models\mathit{Inv}^{2}(l_{3}^{2})
. Because
\mathit{Clk}^{1}\cap\mathit{Clk}^{2}=\emptyset
, it holds that
v^{2}+d[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{2}}\not\models\mathit{Inv}^{2}(l_{3}^{2})
. Therefore, it follows from Definition
3
of the semantic of a TIOA that
(l_{1}^{2},v^{2}+d^{\prime})\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!\llbracket A^{2}\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. Now, from Definition
13
of the conjunction for TIOTS it follows that
((l_{1}^{1},v^{1}+d^{\prime}),(l_{1}^{2},v^{2}+d^{\prime}))\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!Y}
.

*

(l_{3},v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c})\in Q
. In this case, assume that
v+d^{\prime}\models\varphi
and
v+d[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}\models\mathit{Inv}^{A^{1}\wedge A^{2}}(l_{3})
(otherwise, one of the above cases can be used instead). Because
\mathit{Clk}^{1}\cap\mathit{Clk}^{2}=\emptyset
, it follows that
v^{2}+d^{\prime}\models\varphi^{2}
and
v^{2}+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{2}}\models\mathit{Inv}^{2}(l_{3}^{2})
. Therefore, it follows from Definition
3
of the semantic of a TIOA that
(l_{1}^{2},v^{2}+d^{\prime})\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!\llbracket A^{2}\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}(l_{3}^{2},v^{2}+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{2}})
. Now, from Definition
13
of the conjunction for TIOTS it follows that
((l_{1}^{1},v^{1}+d^{\prime}),(l_{1}^{2},v^{2}+d^{\prime}))\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!Y}((l_{1}^{1},v^{1}+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{1}}),(l_{3}^{2},v^{2}+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{2}}))
. And note that
((l_{1}^{1},v^{1}+d^{\prime}),(l_{3}^{2},v^{2}+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{2}}))=(l_{1}^{1},l_{3}^{2},v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c})=(l_{3},v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c})
.

So, in the first two cases we have shown that
((l_{1}^{1},v^{1}+d^{\prime}),(l_{1}^{2},v^{2}+d^{\prime}))\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!Y}
and in the third case that
((l_{1}^{1},v^{1}+d^{\prime}),(l_{1}^{2},v^{2}+d^{\prime}))\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!Y}(l_{3},v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c})
.

So, in all three cases we have shown that
((l_{1}^{1},v^{1}+d^{\prime}),(l_{1}^{2},v^{2}+d^{\prime}))\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!Y}
or
((l_{1}^{1},v^{1}+d^{\prime}),(l_{1}^{2},v^{2}+d^{\prime}))\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!Y}(l_{3},v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c})
. And note that
((l_{1}^{1},v^{1}+d^{\prime}),(l_{1}^{2},v^{2}+d^{\prime}))=q_{2}
and
(l_{3},v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c})=q_{3}
.

So we have shown that
((l_{1}^{1},v^{1}+d^{\prime}),(l_{1}^{2},v^{2}+d^{\prime}))\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!Y}
or
((l_{1}^{1},v^{1}+d^{\prime}),(l_{1}^{2},v^{2}+d^{\prime}))\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!Y}(l_{3},v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c})
with
(l_{3},v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c})\in Q
. We can rewrite this into
q^{X}\xlongrightarrow{d^{\prime}}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{2}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!Y}
or
q^{X}\xlongrightarrow{d^{\prime}}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{3}
. Since we have chosen
d^{\prime}
,
q_{2}
,
q_{3}
, and
o!
arbitrarily, the conclusion holds for all
d^{\prime}
,
q_{2}
,
q_{3}
, and
o!
. Therefore, the second condition in the definition of error states hold for
q^{X}
.

Now, since both conditions in the definition of the error states hold for
q^{X}
, we know that
q^{X}\in\mathrm{err}^{Y}(Q)
. Since we have chosen
q^{X}
arbitrarily from
\mathrm{err}^{X}(Q)
, it holds for all
q^{X}\in\mathrm{err}^{X}(Q)
. Therefore, it holds that
\mathrm{err}^{X}(Q)\subseteq\mathrm{err}^{Y}(Q)
.

(
\mathrm{err}^{Y}\subseteq\mathrm{err}^{X}
)
Consider a state
q^{Y}\in\mathrm{err}^{Y}
. From Definition
11
of error states we know that
\exists d\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
s.t.
q^{Y}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d^{\prime}}{}^{\!\!Y}
and
\forall d^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\forall o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}\forall q_{2}\in Q^{Y}:q^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{d}q_{2}\Rightarrow(q_{2}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!Y}\vee\forall q_{3}\in Q^{Y}:q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{3}\Rightarrow q_{3}\in Q)
. From Definition
13
of the conjunction for TIOTS it follows that
q^{Y}=(q^{\llbracket A^{1}\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}},q^{\llbracket A^{2}\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}})
and
q_{2}=(q_{2}^{\llbracket A^{1}\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}},q_{2}^{\llbracket A^{2}\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}})
.

First, consider the first condition in the definition of error states. From Lemma
4
it follows immediately that
q^{Y}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!Y}
implies that
q^{Y}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X}
. So the first condition in the definition of error states holds for
q^{Y}
in
X
.

Now, consider the second condition in the definition of error states. Pick any
d^{\prime}
,
q_{2}
, and
o!
such that
q^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{d^{\prime}}q_{2}\Rightarrow(q_{2}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!Y}\vee\forall q_{3}\in Q^{Y}:q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{3}\Rightarrow q_{3}\in Q)
. The implication holds if
q^{Y}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d^{\prime}}{}^{\!\!Y}
or
q^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{d^{\prime}}q_{2}\wedge(q_{2}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!Y}\vee\forall q_{3}\in Q^{Y}:q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{3}\Rightarrow q_{3}\in Q)
. The first case follows directly from Lemma
4
that shows that

q^{Y}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!Y}
implies that
q^{Y}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X}
, which ensures that the second condition in the definition of error states holds for
q^{Y}
in
X
. For the second case we again use Lemma
4
, thus
q^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{d^{\prime}}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}
, where
q^{Y}=(l_{1}^{1},l_{1}^{2},v)
and
q_{2}=(l_{1}^{1},l_{1}^{2},v+d)
.

It remains to be shown that
q_{2}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!Y}\vee\forall q_{3}\in Q^{Y}:q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{3}\Rightarrow q_{3}\in Q
in
Y
implies that
q_{2}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}\vee\forall q_{3}\in Q^{X}:q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}\Rightarrow q_{3}\in Q
in
X
. We have to consider the three cases from Definition
13
of the conjunction for TIOTS.

•

o!\in\mathit{Act}^{1}\cap\mathit{Act}^{2}
. It follows directly from Lemma
5
that
q_{2}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}\vee\forall q_{3}\in Q^{X}:q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}\Rightarrow q_{3}\in Q
.

•

o!\in\mathit{Act}^{1}\setminus\mathit{Act}^{2}
. Using Definition
3
of the semantic of a TIOA, we now know that
\nexists(l_{1}^{1},o!,\varphi^{1},c^{1},l_{3}^{1})\in E^{1}
or
\forall(l_{1}^{1},o!,\varphi^{1},c^{1},l_{3}^{1})\in E^{1}:v^{1}+d^{\prime}\not\models\varphi^{1}\vee v^{1}+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{1}}\not\models\mathit{Inv}^{1}(l_{3}^{1})\vee(l_{3}^{1},l_{1}^{2},v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{1}})\in Q
.

In case that
\nexists(l_{1}^{1},o!,\varphi^{1},c^{1},l_{3}^{1})\in E^{1}
, it follows directly from Definition
14
of the conjunction for TIOA that
\nexists((l_{1}^{1},l_{1}^{2}),o!,\varphi^{1},c^{1},(l_{3}^{1},l_{3}^{2}))\in E^{A^{1}\wedge A^{2}}
. Then, with Definition
3
of the semantic of a TIOA, it follows that
(l_{1}^{1},l_{1}^{2},v+d^{\prime})\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}
.

In case that
\forall(l_{1}^{1},o!,\varphi^{1},c^{1},l_{3}^{1})\in E^{1}:v^{1}+d^{\prime}\not\models\varphi^{1}\vee v^{1}+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{1}}\not\models\mathit{Inv}^{1}(l_{3}^{1})
, it follows from Definition
14
that for each edge
(l_{1}^{1},o!,\varphi^{1},c^{1},l_{3}^{1})\in E^{1}
,
\exists((l_{1}^{1},l_{1}^{2}),o!,\varphi^{1},c^{1},(l_{3}^{1},l_{3}^{2}))\in E^{A^{1}\wedge A^{2}}
. Because
\mathit{Clk}^{1}\cap\mathit{Clk}^{2}=\emptyset
, it holds that
v+d^{\prime}\not\models\varphi^{1}\vee v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}\not\models\mathit{Inv}^{1}(l_{3}^{1})
. Therefore, it also holds that
v+d^{\prime}\not\models\varphi^{1}\vee v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}\not\models\mathit{Inv}^{1}(l_{3}^{1})\wedge\mathit{Inv}^{2}(l_{1}^{2})
. Note that from Definition
14
we know that
\mathit{Inv}^{A^{1}\wedge A^{2}}((l_{3}^{1},l_{1}^{2}))=\mathit{Inv}^{1}(l_{3}^{1})\wedge\mathit{Inv}^{2}(l_{1}^{2})
. As we have shown that
v+d^{\prime}\not\models\varphi^{1}\vee v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}\not\models\mathit{Inv}^{1}(l_{3}^{1})\wedge\mathit{Inv}^{2}(l_{1}^{2})
for all edges labeled with
o!
from
(l_{1}^{1},l_{1}^{2})
, it follows from Definition
3
of the semantic of a TIOA that
(l_{1}^{1},l_{1}^{2},v+d^{\prime})\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}
.

In case that
\forall(l_{1}^{1},o!,\varphi^{1},c^{1},l_{3}^{1})\in E^{1}:(l_{3}^{1},l_{1}^{2},v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{1}})\in Q
, it follows from Definition
14
that for each edge
(l_{1}^{1},o!,\varphi^{1},c^{1},l_{3}^{1})\in E^{1}
,
\exists((l_{1}^{1},l_{1}^{2}),o!,\varphi^{1},c^{1},(l_{3}^{1},l_{3}^{2}))\in E^{A^{1}\wedge A^{2}}
. Because
\mathit{Clk}^{1}\cap\mathit{Clk}^{2}=\emptyset
, it holds that
v+d^{\prime}\models\varphi^{1}\wedge v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}\models\mathit{Inv}^{1}(l_{3}^{1})
(in case one of them does not hold, we can use the argument above). Therefore, it also holds that
v+d^{\prime}\models\varphi^{1}\wedge v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}\models\mathit{Inv}^{1}(l_{3}^{1})\wedge\mathit{Inv}^{2}(l_{1}^{2})
. Note that from Definition
14
we know that
\mathit{Inv}^{A^{1}\wedge A^{2}}((l_{3}^{1},l_{1}^{2}))=\mathit{Inv}^{1}(l_{3}^{1})\wedge\mathit{Inv}^{2}(l_{1}^{2})
. As we have shown that
v+d^{\prime}\models\varphi^{1}\wedge v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}\models\mathit{Inv}^{1}(l_{3}^{1})\wedge\mathit{Inv}^{2}(l_{1}^{2})
for all edges labeled with
o!
from
(l_{1}^{1},l_{1}^{2})
, it follows from Definition
3
of the semantic of a TIOA that
(l_{1}^{1},l_{1}^{2},v+d^{\prime})\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}(l_{3}^{1},l_{1}^{2},v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c})
. Now notice that
(l_{3}^{1},l_{1}^{2},v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c})\in Q
.

•

o!\in\mathit{Act}^{2}\setminus\mathit{Act}^{1}
. Using Definition
3
of the semantic of a TIOA, we now know that
\nexists(l_{1}^{2},o!,\varphi^{2},c^{2},l_{3}^{2})\in E^{2}
or
\forall(l_{1}^{2},o!,\varphi^{2},c^{2},l_{3}^{2})\in E^{2}:v^{2}+d^{\prime}\not\models\varphi^{2}\vee v^{2}+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{2}}\not\models\mathit{Inv}^{2}(l_{3}^{2})\vee(l_{1}^{1},l_{3}^{2},v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{1}})\in Q
.

In case that
\nexists(l_{1}^{2},o!,\varphi^{2},c^{2},l_{3}^{2})\in E^{2}
, it follows directly from Definition
14
of the conjunction for TIOA that
\nexists((l_{1}^{1},l_{1}^{2}),o!,\varphi^{2},c^{2},(l_{3}^{1},l_{3}^{2}))\in E^{A^{1}\wedge A^{2}}
. Then, with Definition
3
of the semantic of a TIOA, it follows that
(l_{1}^{1},l_{1}^{2},v+d^{\prime})\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}
.

In case that
\forall(l_{1}^{2},o!,\varphi^{2},c^{2},l_{3}^{2})\in E^{2}:v^{2}+d^{\prime}\not\models\varphi^{2}\vee v^{2}+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{2}}\not\models\mathit{Inv}^{2}(l_{3}^{2})
, it follows from Definition
14
that for each edge
(l_{1}^{2},o!,\varphi^{2},c^{2},l_{3}^{2})\in E^{2}
,
\exists((l_{1}^{1},l_{1}^{2}),o!,\varphi^{2},c^{2},(l_{3}^{1},l_{3}^{2}))\in E^{A^{1}\wedge A^{2}}
. Because
\mathit{Clk}^{1}\cap\mathit{Clk}^{2}=\emptyset
, it holds that
v+d^{\prime}\not\models\varphi^{2}\vee v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}\not\models\mathit{Inv}^{2}(l_{3}^{2})
. Therefore, it also holds that
v+d^{\prime}\not\models\varphi^{2}\vee v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}\not\models\mathit{Inv}^{2}(l_{3}^{2})\wedge\mathit{Inv}^{2}(l_{3}^{2})
. Note that from Definition
14
we know that
\mathit{Inv}^{A^{1}\wedge A^{2}}((l_{3}^{1},l_{1}^{2}))=\mathit{Inv}^{1}(l_{3}^{1})\wedge\mathit{Inv}^{2}(l_{1}^{2})
. As we have shown that
v+d^{\prime}\not\models\varphi^{2}\vee v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}\not\models\mathit{Inv}^{1}(l_{1}^{1})\wedge\mathit{Inv}^{2}(l_{3}^{2})
for all edges labeled with
o!
from
(l_{1}^{1},l_{1}^{2})
, it follows from Definition
3
of the semantic of a TIOA that
(l_{1}^{1},l_{1}^{2},v+d^{\prime})\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}
.

In case that
\forall(l_{1}^{2},o!,\varphi^{2},c^{2},l_{3}^{2})\in E^{2}:(l_{1}^{1},l_{3}^{2},v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{2}})\in Q
, it follows from Definition
14
that for each edge
(l_{1}^{2},o!,\varphi^{2},c^{2},l_{3}^{2})\in E^{2}
,
\exists((l_{1}^{1},l_{1}^{2}),o!,\varphi^{2},c^{2},(l_{3}^{1},l_{2}^{2}))\in E^{A^{1}\wedge A^{2}}
. Because
\mathit{Clk}^{1}\cap\mathit{Clk}^{2}=\emptyset
, it holds that
v+d^{\prime}\models\varphi^{2}\wedge v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}\models\mathit{Inv}^{2}(l_{3}^{2})
(in case one of them does not hold, we can use the argument above). Therefore, it also holds that
v+d^{\prime}\models\varphi^{2}\wedge v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}\models\mathit{Inv}^{1}(l_{1}^{1})\wedge\mathit{Inv}^{2}(l_{3}^{2})
. Note that from Definition
14
we know that
\mathit{Inv}^{A^{1}\wedge A^{2}}((l_{1}^{1},l_{3}^{2}))=\mathit{Inv}^{1}(l_{1}^{1})\wedge\mathit{Inv}^{2}(l_{3}^{2})
. As we have shown that
v+d^{\prime}\models\varphi^{2}\wedge v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}\models\mathit{Inv}^{1}(l_{1}^{1})\wedge\mathit{Inv}^{2}(l_{3}^{2})
for all edges labeled with
o!
from
(l_{1}^{1},l_{1}^{2})
, it follows from Definition
3
of the semantic of a TIOA that
(l_{1}^{1},l_{1}^{2},v+d^{\prime})\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}(l_{1}^{1},l_{3}^{2},v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c})
. Now notice that
(l_{1}^{1},l_{3}^{2},v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c})\in Q
.

So, in all three cases, we have shown that
(l_{1}^{1},l_{1}^{2},v+d^{\prime})\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}
or
((l_{1}^{1},v^{1}+d^{\prime}),(l_{1}^{2},v^{2}+d^{\prime}))\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}(l_{3},v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c})
with
(l_{3},v+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c})\in Q
. We can rewrite this into
q^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{d^{\prime}}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}
or
q^{X}\xlongrightarrow{d^{\prime}}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{3}
. Since we have chosen
d^{\prime}
,
q_{2}
,
q_{3}
, and
o!
arbitrarily, the conclusion holds for all
d^{\prime}
,
q_{2}
,
q_{3}
, and
o!
. Therefore, the second condition in the definition of error states hold for
q^{Y}
.

Now, since both conditions in the definition of the error states hold for
q^{Y}
, we know that
q^{Y}\in\mathrm{err}^{X}
. Since we have chosen
q^{Y}
arbitrarily, it holds for all
q^{Y}\in\mathrm{err}^{Y}
. Therefore, it holds that
\mathrm{err}^{Y}\subseteq\mathrm{err}^{X}
.

∎

Lemma
8
.

Given two TIOAs
A^{i}=(\mathit{Loc}^{i},l_{0}^{i},\mathit{Act}^{i},\mathit{Clk}^{i},E^{i},\mathit{Inv}^{i}),i=1,2
where
\mathit{Act}_{i}^{1}\cap\mathit{Act}_{o}^{2}=\emptyset\wedge\mathit{Act}_{o}^{1}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{2}=\emptyset
. Then
\mathrm{cons}^{\llbracket A^{1}\wedge A^{2}\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=\mathrm{cons}^{\llbracket A^{1}\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\wedge\llbracket A^{2}\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
.

Proof.

We will proof this by using the
\Theta
operator. It follows from Lemma
3
that
\llbracket A^{1}\wedge A^{2}\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
and
\llbracket A^{1}\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\wedge\llbracket A^{2}\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
have the same state set. Also, observe that the semantic of a TIOA, conjunction, and adversarial pruning do not alter the action set. Therefore, it follows that
\llbracket A^{1}\wedge A^{2}\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
and
\llbracket A^{1}\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\wedge\llbracket A^{2}\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
have the same action set and partitioning into input and output actions. We will show for any postfixed point
P
of
\Theta
that
\Theta^{\llbracket A^{1}\wedge A^{2}\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}(P)\subseteq\Theta^{\llbracket A^{1}\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\wedge\llbracket A^{2}\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}(P)
and
\Theta^{\llbracket A^{1}\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\wedge\llbracket A^{2}\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}(P)\subseteq\Theta^{\llbracket A^{1}\wedge A^{2}\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}(P)
. For brevity, we write
X=\llbracket A^{1}\wedge A^{2}\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
,
Y=\llbracket A^{1}\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\wedge\llbracket A^{2}\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
, and
\mathit{Clk}=\mathit{Clk}^{1}\uplus\mathit{Clk}^{2}
in the rest of this proof. Also, we will use
v^{1}
and
v^{2}
to indicate the part of a valuation
v
of only the clocks of
A^{1}
and
A^{2}
, respectively.

(
\Theta^{X}(P)\subseteq\Theta^{Y}(P)
)
Consider a state
q^{X}\in P
. Because
P
is a postfixed point of
\Theta^{X}
, it follows that
q^{X}\in\Theta^{X}(P)
. From the definition of
\Theta
, it follows that
q^{X}\in\overline{\mathrm{err}^{X}(\overline{P})}
and
q^{X}\in\{q_{1}\in Q^{X}\mid\forall d\geq 0:[\forall q_{2}\in Q^{X}:q_{1}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}\Rightarrow q_{2}\in P\wedge\forall i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}:\exists q_{3}\in P:q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}]\ \vee[\exists d^{\prime}\leq d\wedge\exists q_{2},q_{3}\in P\wedge\exists o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{X}:q_{1}\xlongrightarrow{d^{\prime}}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}\wedge q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}\wedge\forall i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}:\exists q_{4}\in P:q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{4}]\}
. From Lemma
7
it follows directly that
q^{X}\in\overline{\mathrm{err}^{Y}(\overline{P})}
. Now we only focus on the second part of the definition of
\Theta
.

Consider a
d\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
. Then the left-hand side or the right-hand side of the disjunction is true (or both).

•

Assume the left-hand side is true, i.e.,
\forall q_{2}\in Q^{X}:q^{X}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}\Rightarrow q_{2}\in P\wedge\forall i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}:\exists q_{3}\in P:q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}
. Pick a
q_{2}\in Q^{X}
. The implication is true when
q^{X}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}
or
q^{X}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}\wedge q_{2}\in P\wedge\forall i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}:\exists q_{3}\in P:q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}
.

–

Consider the first case. From Lemma
4
it follows that
q^{X}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!Y}
. Note that
q^{X}=(l_{1}^{1},v_{1}^{1},l_{1}^{2},v_{1}^{2})
. Thus the implication also holds for
q_{2}
in
Y
.

–

Consider the second case. From Lemma
4
, we have that
q^{X}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}
implies that
q^{X}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{2}
, and from Definition
3
of the semantic of a TIOA it follows that
v_{1}+d\models\mathit{Inv}^{A^{1}\wedge A^{2}}(l_{1})
for
q^{X}=(l_{1},v_{1})
,
q_{2}=(l_{1},v_{1}+d)
,
l_{1}\in\mathit{Loc}^{A^{1}\wedge A^{2}}
, and
v_{1}\in[\mathit{Clk}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]
. Now, pick
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}
and
q_{3}\in Q^{X}
such that
q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}
and
q_{3}\in P
. From Definition
3
of the semantic of a TIOA it follows that
(l_{1},i?,\varphi,c,l_{3})\in E^{A^{1}\wedge A^{2}}
,
q_{3}=(l_{3},v_{3})
,
v_{1}+d\models\varphi
,
v_{3}=v_{1}+d[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}
, and
v_{3}\models\mathit{Inv}^{A^{1}\wedge A^{2}}(l_{3})
. From Definition
14
of the conjunction of TIOA it follows that
l_{1}=(l_{1}^{1},l_{1}^{2})
,
l_{3}=(l_{3}^{1},l_{3}^{2})
,
\mathit{Inv}^{A^{1}\wedge A^{2}}(l_{1})=\mathit{Inv}^{1}(l_{1}^{1})\wedge\mathit{Inv}^{2}(l_{1}^{2})
, and
\mathit{Inv}^{A^{1}\wedge A^{2}}(l_{3})=\mathit{Inv}^{1}(l_{3}^{1})\wedge\mathit{Inv}^{2}(l_{3}^{2})
. We have to consider the three cases of Definition
14
in relation to
i?
.

*

i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{1}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{2}
. It follows directly from Lemma
5
that
q_{2}\xrightarrow{i?}q_{3}
is a transition in
Y
.

*

i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{1}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{2}
. It follows directly from Corollary
1
that
q_{2}\xrightarrow{i?}q_{3}
is a transition in
Y
.

*

i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{2}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{1}
. It follows directly from Corollary
1
(where we switched
A^{1}
and
A^{2}
) that
q_{2}\xrightarrow{i?}q_{3}
is a transition in
Y
.

So, in all three cases we have that
q_{2}\xrightarrow{i?}q_{3}
is a transition in
Y
. As the analysis above is independent of the particular
i?
,
q_{2}\xrightarrow{i?}q_{3}
is a transition in
Y
for all
i?
. Because both
q_{2},q_{3}\in P
and
q^{X}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{2}
, we have that the implication also holds for
q_{2}\in Y
.

So, in both cases we have that for
q^{X}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{2}\Rightarrow q_{2}\in P\wedge\forall i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{Y}:\exists q_{3}\in P:q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{3}
. As
q_{2}
is chosen arbitrarily, it holds for all
q_{2}\in Q^{X}=Q^{Y}
. Therefore, the left-hand side is true.

•

Assume the right-hand side is true, i.e.,
\exists d^{\prime}\leq d\wedge\exists q_{2},q_{3}\in P\wedge\exists o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{X}:q^{X}\xlongrightarrow{d^{\prime}}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}\wedge q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}\wedge\forall i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}:\exists q_{4}\in P:q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{4}
.

First, following Definition
3
of the semantic of a TIOA, we have that
q^{X}=(l_{1},v_{1})
,
q_{2}=(l_{1},v_{1}+d^{\prime})
,
q_{3}=(l_{3},v_{3})
,
q_{4}=(l_{4},v_{4})
,
l_{1},l_{3},l_{4}\in\mathit{Loc}^{A^{1}\wedge A^{2}}
,
v_{1},v_{3},v_{4}\in[\mathit{Clk}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]
,
v_{1}+d^{\prime}\models\mathit{Inv}^{A^{1}\wedge A^{2}}(l_{1})
,
\exists(l_{1},o!,\varphi,c,l_{3})\in E^{A^{1}\wedge A^{2}}
,
v_{1}+d^{\prime}\models\varphi
,
v_{3}=v_{1}+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}
, and
v_{3}\models\mathit{Inv}^{A^{1}\wedge A^{2}}(l_{3})
. First, focus on the delay transition. From Lemma
4
it follows that
q^{X}\xlongrightarrow{d^{\prime}}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{2}
in
Y
, with
q^{X}=(l_{1}^{1},v_{1}^{1},l_{1}^{2},v_{1}^{2})=(l_{1}^{1},l_{1}^{2},v_{1})
and
q_{2}=(l_{1}^{1},v_{1}^{1}+d^{\prime},l_{1}^{2},v_{1}^{2}+d^{\prime})=(l_{1}^{1},l_{1}^{2},v_{1}+d^{\prime})
.

Now consider the output transition labeled with
o!
. We have to consider the three cases from Definition
14
.

–

o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{1}\cap\mathit{Act}_{o}^{2}
. It follows directly from Lemma
5
that
q_{2}\xrightarrow{o!}q_{3}
is a transition in
Y
.

–

o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{1}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{2}
. It follows directly from Corollary
1
that
q_{2}\xrightarrow{o!}q_{3}
is a transition in
Y
.

–

o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{2}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{1}
. It follows directly from Corollary
1
(where we switched
A^{1}
and
A^{2}
) that
q_{2}\xrightarrow{o!}q_{3}
is a transition in
Y
.

Thus, in all three cases we have that
q_{2}\xrightarrow{o!}q_{3}
is a transition in
Y
. Therefore, we can conclude that
{q^{X}\xlongrightarrow{d^{\prime}}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{2}}\wedge q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{3}
with
q_{2},q_{3}\in P
.

Finally, consider the input transitions labeled with
i?
. Using the same argument as before, we can show that
q_{2}\xrightarrow{i?}q_{4}
in
X
is also a transition in
Y
, and
q_{4}\in P
. Therefore, we can conclude that
{q^{X}\xlongrightarrow{d^{\prime}}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{2}}\wedge q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{3}\wedge\forall i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{Y}:\exists q_{4}\in P:q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{4}
with
q_{2},q_{3},q_{4}\in P
. Thus, the right-hand side is true.

Thus, we have shown that when the left-hand side is true for
q^{X}
in
X
, it is also true for
q^{X}
in
Y
; and that when the right-hand side is true for
q^{X}
in
X
, it is also true for
q^{X}
in
Y
. Thus,
q^{X}\in\Theta^{Y}(P)
. Since
q^{X}\in P
was chosen arbitrarily, it holds for all states in
P
. Once we choose
P
to be the fixed-point of
\Theta^{X}
, we have that
\Theta^{X}(P)\subseteq\Theta^{Y}(P)
.

(
\Theta^{Y}(P)\subseteq\Theta^{X}(P)
)
Consider a state
q^{Y}\in P
. Because
P
is a postfixed point of
\Theta^{Y}
, it follows that
p\in\Theta^{X}(Y)
. From the definition of
\Theta
, it follows that
q^{Y}\in\overline{\mathrm{err}^{Y}(\overline{P})}
and
q^{Y}\in\{q\in Q^{Y}\mid\forall d\geq 0:[\forall q_{2}\in Q^{Y}:q\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{2}\Rightarrow q_{2}\in P\wedge\forall i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{Y}:\exists q_{3}\in P:q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{3}]\ \vee[\exists d^{\prime}\leq d\wedge\exists q_{2},q_{3}\in P\wedge\exists o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{Y}:q\xlongrightarrow{d^{\prime}}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{2}\wedge q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{3}\wedge\forall i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}:\exists q_{4}\in P:q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{4}]\}
. From Lemma
7
it follows directly that
q^{X}\in\overline{\mathrm{err}^{X}(\overline{P})}
. Now we only focus on the second part of the definition of
\Theta
.

Consider a
d\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
. Then the left-hand side or the right-hand side of the disjunction is true (or both).

•

Assume the left-hand side is true, i.e.,
\forall q_{2}\in Q^{Y}:q^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{2}\Rightarrow q_{2}\in P\wedge\forall i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{Y}:\exists q_{3}\in P:q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{3}
. Pick a
q_{2}\in Q^{Y}
. The implication is true when
q^{Y}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{2}
or
q^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{2}\wedge q_{2}\in P\wedge\forall i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{Y}:\exists q_{3}\in P:q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{3}
.

–

Consider the first case. From Lemma
4
it follows that
q^{Y}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X}
. Note that
q^{Y}=(l^{1},v^{1},l^{2},v^{2})
. Thus the implication also holds for
q_{2}
in
X
.

–

Consider the second case. From Lemma
4
we have that
q^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{2}
implies that
q^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}
, and from Definition
13
of the conjunction for TIOTS that
q^{Y}=(q_{1}^{1},q_{1}^{2})
and
q_{2}=(q_{2}^{1},q_{2}^{2})
. Also, using Definition
3
of the semantic of a TIOA it follows for
i=1,2
that
q_{1}^{i}=(l_{1}^{i},v_{1}^{i})
,
q_{2}^{i}=(l_{1}^{i},v_{1}^{i}+d)
,
l_{1}^{i}\in\mathit{Loc}^{i}
, and
v_{1}^{i}\in[\mathit{Clk}^{i}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]
.
Now, pick an
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{Y}
with its corresponding
q_{3}
according to the implication. We have to consider the three cases from Definition
13
.

*

i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{1}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{2}
. It follows directly from Lemma
5
that
q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}
.

*

i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{1}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{2}
. From the fact that
q^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}
12
12

12

This fact is key for finalizing the proof of Theorem
7
: without adversarial pruning in that theorem, you cannot assume this, and you get stuck in proving that
v_{3}\models\mathit{Inv}^{2}(l_{1}^{2})
and thus
v_{3}\models\mathit{Inv}^{A^{1}\wedge A^{2}}((l_{3}^{1},l_{1}^{2}))
, i.e., you cannot prove that.
, it follows from Definitions

and

that
v_{1}^{2}+d\models\mathit{Inv}^{2}(l_{1}^{2})
(see also proof of Lemma

). Observe that
v_{1}^{2}+d[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{1}}=v_{1}^{2}+d
, so
v_{3}\models\mathit{Inv}^{2}(l_{1}^{2})
. Now it follows directly from Lemma
6
that
q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}
.

*

i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{2}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{1}
. From the fact that
q^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}
, it follows from Definitions
3
and
13
that
v_{1}^{1}+d\models\mathit{Inv}^{1}(l_{1}^{1})
(see also proof of Lemma

). Observe that

, so
v_{3}\models\mathit{Inv}^{1}(l_{1}^{1})
. Now it follows directly from Lemma
6
(where we switched
A^{2}
and
A^{2}
) that
q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}
.

Thus, in all three cases we can show that
q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{3}
implies
q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}
. Since we have chosen an arbitrarily
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{Y}
, it holds for all
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{Y}
. Thus the implication also holds for
q_{2}
in
X
.

Thus, in both cases the implication holds. Therefore, we can conclude that
q^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}\Rightarrow q_{2}\in P\wedge\forall i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}:\exists q_{3}\in P:q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}
. As
q_{2}
is chosen arbitrarily, it holds for all
q_{2}\in Q^{X}=Q^{Y}
. Therefore, the left-hand side is true.

•

Assume the right-hand side is true, i.e.,
\exists d^{\prime}\leq d\wedge\exists q_{2},q_{3}\in P\wedge\exists o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{Y}:q\xlongrightarrow{d^{\prime}}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{2}\wedge q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{3}\wedge\forall i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}:\exists q_{4}\in P:q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{4}
. First, focus on the delay. From Lemma
4
it follows that
q\xlongrightarrow{d^{\prime}}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{2}
implies
q\xlongrightarrow{d^{\prime}}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}
, and from Definition
13
of the conjunction for TIOTS that
q^{Y}=(q_{1}^{1},q_{1}^{2})
and
q_{2}=(q_{2}^{1},q_{2}^{2})
. Also, using Definition
3
of the semantic of a TIOA it follows for
i=1,2
that
q_{1}^{i}=(l_{1}^{i},v_{1}^{i})
,
q_{2}^{i}=(l_{1}^{i},v_{1}^{i}+d^{\prime})
,
l_{1}^{i}\in\mathit{Loc}^{i}
, and
v_{1}^{i}\in[\mathit{Clk}^{i}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]
. Now, consider the output transition labeled with
o!
. We have to consider the three cases from Definition
13
of the conjunction for TIOTS.

–

o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{1}\cap\mathit{Act}_{o}^{2}
. It follows directly from Lemma
5
that
q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}
.

–

o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{1}\subset\mathit{Act}_{o}^{2}
. From the fact that
q^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{d^{\prime}}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}
, it follows from Definitions
3
and
13
that
v_{1}^{2}+d^{\prime}\models\mathit{Inv}^{2}(l_{1}^{2})
(see also proof of Lemma

). Observe that
v_{1}^{2}+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{1}}=v_{1}^{2}+d^{\prime}
, so
v_{3}\models\mathit{Inv}^{2}(l_{1}^{2})
. Now it follows directly from Lemma
6
that
q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}
.

–

o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{2}\subset\mathit{Act}_{o}^{1}
. From the fact that
q^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{d^{\prime}}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}
, it follows from Definitions
3
and
13
that
v_{1}^{1}+d^{\prime}\models\mathit{Inv}^{1}(l_{1}^{1})
(see also proof of Lemma

). Observe that
v_{1}^{1}+d^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{2}}=v_{1}^{1}+d^{\prime}
, so
v_{3}\models\mathit{Inv}^{1}(l_{1}^{1})
. Now it follows directly from Lemma
6
(where we switched
A^{2}
and
A^{2}
) that
q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}
.

Thus, in all three cases we have that
q_{2}\xrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}
is a transition in
X
. Therefore, we can conclude that
{q^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{d^{\prime}}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}}\wedge q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}
with
q_{2},q_{3}\in P
. Thus, the right-hand side is true.

Finally, consider the input transitions labeled with
i?
. Using the same argument as before, we can show that
q_{2}\xrightarrow{i?}q_{4}
in

is also a transition in

, and

. Therefore, we can conclude that
{q^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{d^{\prime}}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}}\wedge q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}\wedge\forall i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}:\exists q_{4}\in P:q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{4}
with
q_{2},q_{3},q_{4}\in P
. Thus, the right-hand side is true.

Thus, we have shown that when the left-hand side is true for
q^{Y}
in

, it is also true for
q^{Y}
in

; and that when the right-hand side is true for
q^{Y}
in

, it is also true for
q^{Y}
in

. Thus,
q^{Y}\in\Theta^{X}(P)
. Since
q^{Y}\in P
was chosen arbitrarily, it holds for all states in
P
. Once we choose
P
to be the fixed-point of
\Theta^{Y}
, we have that
\Theta^{Y}(P)\subseteq\Theta^{X}(P)
.

∎

8.3
Omitted proofs of Section
5

Lemma
9
.

Given two locally consistent specifications
S^{i}=(Q^{i},q_{0}^{i},\mathit{Act}^{i},\rightarrow^{i}),i=1,2
where
\mathit{Act}_{o}^{1}\cap\mathit{Act}_{o}^{2}=\emptyset
. Then
S^{1}\parallel S^{2}
is locally consistent.

Proof.

Since,
S^{1}
and
S^{2}
are locally consistent, the only reason why
S^{1}\parallel S^{2}
could be inconsistent is when a new error state is created by the parallel composition. We show by contradiction that this is not possible.

Assume that state
q_{1}\in S^{1}\parallel S^{2}
is an error state. From Definition
11
of the error state it follows that
\exists d_{1}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}:q_{1}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d_{1}}\wedge\forall d_{2}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\forall o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}\forall q_{2}\in Q:q_{1}\xlongrightarrow{d_{2}}q_{2}\Rightarrow q_{2}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}
. From Definition
15
of the parallel composition for TIOTS it follows that (1)
q_{1}=(q_{1}^{1},q_{1}^{2})
with
q_{1}^{1}\in Q^{1}
and
q_{1}^{2}\in Q^{2}
, and that either
q^{1}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d_{1}}{}^{\!\!1}
or
q^{2}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d_{2}}{}^{\!\!2}
(or both); (2) that
q_{2}=(q_{2}^{1},q_{2}^{2})
with
q_{2}^{1}\in Q^{1}
and
q_{2}^{2}\in Q^{2}
, and that
q_{1}^{1}\xlongrightarrow{d_{2}}{}^{\!\!1}q_{2}^{1}
and
q_{1}^{2}\xlongrightarrow{d_{2}}{}^{\!\!2}q_{2}^{2}
; and (3) that
o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{1}
and possibly
o?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{2}
, or
o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{2}
and possibly
o?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{1}
. In the next step we assume that
o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{1}
and possibly
o?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{2}
, as the other case is symmetrical. Consider two cases and Definition
15
:

•

o?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{2}
. As
S^{2}
is a specification, it is input-enabled. Therefore,
q_{1}\xlongrightarrow{d_{2}}q_{2}\Rightarrow q_{2}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}
implies that
q_{1}^{1}\xlongrightarrow{d_{2}}q_{2}^{1}\Rightarrow q_{2}^{1}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}
.

•

o?\notin\mathit{Act}_{i}^{2}
. This directly results in that
q_{1}\xlongrightarrow{d_{2}}q_{2}\Rightarrow q_{2}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}
implies that
q_{1}^{1}\xlongrightarrow{d_{2}}q_{2}^{1}\Rightarrow q_{2}^{1}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}
.

Applying the above reasoning for all output actions and knowing that
\mathit{Act}_{o}=\mathit{Act}_{o}^{1}\cup\mathit{Act}_{o}^{2}
from Definition
15
, it follows that
\forall o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{1}:q_{1}^{1}\xlongrightarrow{d_{2}}{}^{\!\!1}q_{2}^{1}\implies q_{2}^{1}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}
and
\forall o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{2}:q_{1}^{2}\xlongrightarrow{d_{2}}{}^{\!\!2}q_{2}^{2}\implies q_{2}^{2}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}
. As this is independent of the actual value of
d_{2}
, it holds for all
d_{2}
.

Finally, since either
q^{1}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d_{1}}{}^{\!\!1}
or
q^{2}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d_{2}}{}^{\!\!2}
(or both), it follows that either
\exists d_{1}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}:q_{1}^{1}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d_{1}}{}^{\!\!1}\wedge\forall d_{2}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\forall o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{1}\forall q_{2}^{1}\in Q^{1}:q_{1}^{1}\xlongrightarrow{d_{2}}{}^{\!\!1}q_{2}^{1}\Rightarrow q_{2}^{1}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!1}
or
\exists d_{1}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}:q_{1}^{2}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d_{1}}{}^{\!\!2}\wedge\forall d_{2}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\forall o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{2}\forall q_{2}^{2}\in Q^{1}:q_{1}^{2}\xlongrightarrow{d_{2}}{}^{\!\!2}q_{2}^{2}\Rightarrow q_{2}^{2}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!2}
(or both). Therefore, either
q_{1}^{1}
or
q_{1}^{2}
(or both) is an error state, which contradicts with the antecedent stating that
S^{1}
and
S^{2}
are consistent.

∎

Lemma
8
.

Refinement is a pre-congruence with respect to parallel composition: for any specifications
S^{1}
,
S^{2}
, and
T
such that
S^{1}\leq S^{2}
and
S^{1}
is composable with
T
, we have that
S^{2}
is composable with
T
and
S^{1}\parallel T\leq S^{2}\parallel T
.

Proof.

S^{1}\leq S^{2}
implies that
\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S^{2}}\subseteq\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S^{2}}
(see Definition
6
), and
S^{1}
is composable with
T
implies that
\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S^{1}}\cap\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}=\emptyset
. Combining this results immediately in that
\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S^{2}}\cap\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}=\emptyset
, thus
S^{2}
is composable with
T
. Furthermore, since
S^{1}\leq S^{2}
, there exists a relation
R\in Q^{1}\times Q^{2}
with the properties given in Definition
6
of the refinement. Construct relation
R^{\prime}=\{((q^{1},q^{T}),(q^{2},q^{T}))\in Q^{S^{1}\parallel T}\times Q^{S^{2}\parallel T}\mid(q^{1},q^{2})\in R\}
. We show that
R^{\prime}
witnesses
S^{1}\parallel T\leq S^{2}\parallel T
. Consider the five cases of refinement for a state pair
((q_{1}^{1},q_{1}^{T}),(q_{1}^{2},q_{1}^{T}))\in R^{\prime}
.

1.

(q_{1}^{2},q_{1}^{T})\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!S^{2}\parallel T}(q_{2}^{2},q_{2}^{T})
for some
(q_{2}^{2},q_{2}^{T})\in Q^{S^{2}\parallel T}
and
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S^{2}\parallel T}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S^{1}\parallel T}
. Consider the five feasible combinations for input action
i?
using Definition
15
such that
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S^{2}\parallel T}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S^{1}\parallel T}
.

•

i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S^{1}}
,
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S^{2}}
, and
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}
. In this case, it follows from Definition
15
that
q_{1}^{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!S^{2}}q_{2}^{2}
and
q_{1}^{T}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!T}q_{2}^{T}
. Now, using
R
and Definition
6
, it follows that
q_{1}^{1}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!S^{1}}q_{2}^{1}
and
(q_{2}^{1},q_{2}^{2})\in R
. Thus, following Definition
15
again, we have that
(q_{1}^{1},q_{1}^{T})\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!S^{1}\parallel T}(q_{2}^{1},q_{2}^{T})
. From the construction of
R^{\prime}
we confirm that
((q_{2}^{1},q_{2}^{T}),(q_{2}^{2},q_{2}^{T}))\in R^{\prime}
.

•

i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S^{1}}
,
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S^{2}}
, and
i?\notin\mathit{Act}^{T}
. In this case, it follows from Definition
15
that
q_{1}^{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!S^{2}}q_{2}^{2}
and
q_{1}^{T}=q_{2}^{T}
. Now, using
R
and Definition
6
, it follows that
q_{1}^{1}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!S^{1}}q_{2}^{1}
and
(q_{2}^{1},q_{2}^{2})\in R
. Thus, following Definition
15
again, we have that
(q_{1}^{1},q_{1}^{T})\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!S^{1}\parallel T}(q_{2}^{1},q_{2}^{T})
with
q_{1}^{T}=q_{2}^{T}
. From the construction of
R^{\prime}
we confirm that
((q_{2}^{1},q_{2}^{T}),(q_{2}^{2},q_{2}^{T}))\in R^{\prime}
.

•

i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S^{1}}
,
i\notin\mathit{Act}^{S^{2}}
, and
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}
. This case is infeasible, as Definition
6
of refinement requires that
\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S^{1}}\subseteq\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S^{2}}
.

•

i?\notin\mathit{Act}^{S^{1}}
,
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S^{2}}
, and
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}
. In this case, it follows from Definition
15
that
q_{1}^{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!S^{2}}q_{2}^{2}
and
q_{1}^{T}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!T}q_{2}^{T}
. Now, using
R
and Definition
6
, it follows that
(q_{2}^{1},q_{2}^{2})\in R
and
q_{1}^{1}=q_{2}^{1}
. Thus, following Definition
15
again, we have that
(q_{1}^{1},q_{1}^{T})\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!S^{1}\parallel T}(q_{2}^{1},q_{2}^{T})
and
q_{1}^{1}=q_{2}^{1}
. From the construction of
R^{\prime}
we confirm that
((q_{2}^{1},q_{2}^{T}),(q_{2}^{2},q_{2}^{T}))\in R^{\prime}
.

•

i?\notin\mathit{Act}^{S^{1}}
,
i?\notin\mathit{Act}^{S^{2}}
, and
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}
. In this case, it follows from Definition
15
that
q_{1}^{T}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!T}q_{2}^{T}
and
q_{1}^{2}=q_{2}^{2}
. Following Definition
15
again, we have that
(q_{1}^{1},q_{1}^{T})\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!S^{1}\parallel T}(q_{2}^{1},q_{2}^{T})
and
q_{1}^{1}=q_{2}^{1}
. From the construction of
R^{\prime}
we confirm that
((q_{2}^{1},q_{2}^{T}),(q_{2}^{2},q_{2}^{T}))\in R^{\prime}
.

So, in all feasible cases we can show that
(q_{1}^{1},q_{1}^{T})\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!S^{1}\parallel T}(q_{2}^{1},q_{2}^{T})
and
((q_{2}^{1},q_{2}^{T}),(q_{2}^{2},q_{2}^{T}))\in R^{\prime}
.

2.

(q_{1}^{2},q_{1}^{T})\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!S^{2}\parallel T}(q_{2}^{2},q_{2}^{T})
for some
(q_{2}^{2},q_{2}^{T})\in Q^{S^{2}\parallel T}
and
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S^{2}\parallel T}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S^{1}\parallel T}
. In this case it follows from Definition

and
15
that
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S^{2}}
,
i?\notin\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S^{1}}
, and
i?\notin\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}
. Therefore, from the same definitions, we have that
q_{1}^{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!S^{2}}q_{2}^{2}
and
q_{1}^{T}=q_{2}^{T}
. Now, using
R
and Definition
6
, it follows that
(q_{2}^{1},q_{2}^{2})\in R
and
q_{1}^{1}=q_{2}^{1}
. From the construction of
R^{\prime}
we confirm that
((q_{2}^{1},q_{2}^{T}),(q_{2}^{2},q_{2}^{T}))\in R^{\prime}
.

3.

(q_{1}^{1},q_{1}^{T})\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!S^{1}\parallel T}(q_{2}^{1},q_{2}^{T})
for some
(q_{2}^{1},q_{2}^{T})\in Q^{S^{1}\parallel T}
and
o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S^{1}\parallel T}\cap\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S^{2}\parallel T}
. Consider the eight feasible combinations for output action
o!
using Definition
15
such that
o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S^{2}\parallel T}\cap\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S^{1}\parallel T}
, already taking into account that if
o\in\mathit{Act}^{S^{1}}
and
o\in\mathit{Act}^{S^{2}}

o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S^{1}}
and
o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S^{2}}
or
o?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S^{1}}
and
o?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S^{2}}
(see Definition
6
).

•

o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S^{1}}
,
o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S^{2}}
, and
o\in\mathit{Act}^{T}
13
13

13

With this notation, we indicate that it does not matter whether
o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}
or
o?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}
.
. In this case, it follows from Definition
15
that
q_{1}^{1}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!S^{1}}q_{2}^{1}
and
q_{1}^{T}\xlongrightarrow{o}{}^{\!\!T}q_{2}^{T}
. Now, using
R
and Definition

, it follows that
q_{1}^{2}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!S^{2}}q_{2}^{2}
and

. Thus, following Definition

(q_{1}^{2},q_{1}^{T})\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!S^{2}\parallel T}(q_{2}^{2},q_{2}^{T})
. From the construction of
R^{\prime}
we confirm that
((q_{2}^{1},q_{2}^{T}),(q_{2}^{2},q_{2}^{T}))\in R^{\prime}
.

•

o?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S^{1}}

, and

. In this case, it follows from Definition

and

. Now, using

and Definition

, it follows that

. Thus, following Definition

((q_{2}^{1},q_{2}^{T}),(q_{2}^{2},q_{2}^{T}))\in R^{\prime}
.

•

o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S^{1}}

. In this case, it follows from Definition

. From the construction of

((q_{2}^{1},q_{2}^{T}),(q_{2}^{2},q_{2}^{T}))\in R^{\prime}

.

Lemma
10
.

Given a specification
S=(Q^{S},s_{0},\mathit{Act}^{S},\rightarrow^{S})
and its cooperatively pruned subspecification
S^{\forall}
. It holds that
S=S^{\forall}
.

Proof.

The idea of pruning is to remove error states and related transitions from a specification that violate the independent progress property, as all states of any implementation of that specification need to have independent progress, see Definition
5
. So, for a state
q_{\mathrm{imerr}}\in\mathrm{imerr}^{S}
of
S
(see Definition
10
), it holds that
(\exists d\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}:q_{\mathrm{imerr}}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d})\wedge\forall d\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\forall o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\forall q^{\prime}\in Q^{S}:q_{\mathrm{imerr}}\xlongrightarrow{d}q^{\prime}\Rightarrow q^{\prime}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}
.

Now, consider a specification
T=(t,t,\mathit{Act}^{T},\rightarrow^{T})
with a single state
t
,
\mathit{Act}^{T}=\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}=\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\cup\{\tau\}
with
\tau\notin\mathit{Act}^{S}
, and
\rightarrow^{T}=\{(t,a,t)\mid a\in\mathit{Act}^{T}\}\cup\{(t,d,t)\mid d\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\}
. The unique event
\tau
is present to ensure that the following argument holds in case
S
does not have any input actions. In the composition
S\parallel T
, it still holds that
(q_{\mathrm{imerr}},t)\xlongrightarrow{d}(q^{\prime},t)
(see Definition
15
). Since a specification is input enabled, Definition
4
, we know that in the composition
S\parallel T
there exist an output action
o!\in\mathit{Act}^{T}
such that
(q^{\prime},t)\xlongrightarrow{o!}
. Thus, in the composition
S\parallel T
, the state
(q_{\mathrm{imerr}},t)
is no longer an immediate error state. As this holds for all
q_{\mathrm{imerr}}\in\mathrm{imerr}^{S}
, we have that
\mathrm{imerr}^{S\parallel T}=\emptyset
. And once
\mathrm{imerr}^{S\parallel T}=\emptyset
, we have that
\mathrm{err}^{S\parallel T}(\emptyset)=\emptyset
and therefore
\mathrm{incons}^{S\parallel T}=\emptyset
(using the fixed-point operator
\pi
). Thus for this
T
we need to keep all states of
S
in
S^{\forall}
to ensure that
\llbracket S\parallel T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{mod}}=\llbracket S^{\forall}\parallel T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{mod}}
.

∎

8.4
Omitted proofs of Section
6

Lemma 17
.

For any two specifications
S
and
T
such that the quotient
T\backslash\!\backslash S
is defined, and for any implementation
X
over the same alphabet as
T\backslash\!\backslash S
, we have that
S\parallel X
is defined,
\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S\parallel X}=\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}
and
\mathit{Act}_{0}^{S\parallel X}=\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cup\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}\cup\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}
.

Proof.

We will first show that
S\parallel X
is defined. This boils down to show that
S
and
X
are composable., i.e.,
\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{o}^{X}=\emptyset
. From Definition
18
and the assumption that
X
has the same alphabet as
T\backslash\!\backslash S
, it follows that
\mathit{Act}_{o}^{X}=\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cup\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}
. Thus it holds that
\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{o}^{X}=\emptyset
.

To show that
\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S\parallel X}=\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}
, we follow Definition
15
of the parallel composition and Definition
18
of the quotient and use careful rewriting to get to this conclusion.

\displaystyle\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S\parallel X}

\displaystyle=\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{X}\cup\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}

\displaystyle=\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus(\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cup\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T})\cup(\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}\cup\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S})\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}

\displaystyle=\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus(\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cup\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T})\cup\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cup\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}

\displaystyle=\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus(\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cup\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T})\cup\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus(\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S})\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus(\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T})\right)\cup\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus(\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S})\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}\right)\cup\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}

\displaystyle=\left(\left((\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S})\cup(\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T})\right)\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}\right)\cup\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}\right)\cup\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}\right)\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}\cup\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}\right)\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}\cup\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\cap(\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T})\right)\cup\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}\right)\cup\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}

\displaystyle=\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}

To show that
\mathit{Act}_{0}^{S\parallel X}=\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cup\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}\cup\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}
, we follow again Definition
15
of the parallel composition and Definition
18
of the quotient and use careful rewriting to get to this conclusion.

\displaystyle\mathit{Act}_{0}^{S\parallel X}

\displaystyle=\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cup\mathit{Act}_{o}^{X}

\displaystyle=\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cup(\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cup\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}^{T})

\displaystyle=\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cup\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}\cup\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}

∎

Lemma
10
.

For any two specifications
S
and
T
such that the quotient
T\backslash\!\backslash S
is defined, and for any implementation
X
over the same alphabet as
T\backslash\!\backslash S
, we have that
S\parallel X
is defined and
S\parallel X\leq T
iff
X\leq T\backslash\!\backslash S
.

Proof.

It is shown in Lemma
17
that
S\parallel X
is defined. The alphabet pre-condition of Definition
6
is satisfied for
X\leq T\backslash\!\backslash S
by definition of
X
; using Lemma
17
we can see that this is also the case for
S\parallel X\leq T
. So we only have to show that
S\parallel X\leq T
iff
X\leq T\backslash\!\backslash S
.

(
S\parallel X\leq T\Rightarrow X\leq T\backslash\!\backslash S
) Since
S\parallel X\leq T
, it follows from Definition
6
of refinement that there exists a relation
R\in Q^{S\parallel X}\times Q^{T}
that witness the refinement. Note that
Q^{S\parallel X}=Q^{S}\times Q^{X}
according to Definition
15
. Construct relation
R^{\prime}=\{(q_{1}^{X},(q_{1}^{T},q_{1}^{S}))\in Q^{X}\times Q^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\mid((q_{1}^{S},q_{1}^{X}),q_{1}^{T})\in R\}\cup\{(q_{1}^{X},u)\in Q^{X}\times Q^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\mid q_{1}^{X}\in Q^{X}\}
. We will show that
R^{\prime}
witnesses
X\leq T\backslash\!\backslash S
. First consider the five cases of Definition
6
for a state pair
(q_{1}^{X},(q_{1}^{T},q_{1}^{S}))\in R^{\prime}
.

1.

(q_{1}^{T},q_{1}^{S})\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!T\backslash\!\backslash S}(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S})
for some
(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S})\in Q^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
and
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}
. By definition of
X
it follows that
\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}=\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}
. Consider the following five possible cases from Definition
18
of the quotient that might result in
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}(=\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}\cup\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S})
.

•

i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}
and
i!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}
. This case is actually not feasible, since Definition
18
also requires that
\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}=\emptyset
.

•

i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}
and
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}
. In this case, it follows from Definition
18
that
q_{1}^{T}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!T}q_{2}^{T}
and
q_{1}^{S}\ {\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!S}}q_{2}^{S}
. Now, using
R
, the first case of Definition
6
of refinement, and the fact that
\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S\parallel X}=\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}
(Lemma
17
) it follows that
(q_{1}^{S},q_{1}^{X})\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!S\parallel X}(q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X})
and
((q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X}),q_{2}^{T})\in R
. From Definition
15
of parallel composition it follows that
q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}^{X}
. From the construction of
R^{\prime}
we confirm that
(q_{2}^{X},(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S}))\in R^{\prime}
.

•

i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}
and
i?\notin\mathit{Act}^{S}
. In this case, it follows from Definition
18
that
q_{1}^{T}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!T}q_{2}^{T}
and
q_{1}^{S}=q_{2}^{S}
. Now, using
R
, the first case of Definition
6
of refinement, and the fact that
\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S\parallel X}=\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}
(Lemma
17
) it follows that
(q_{1}^{S},q_{1}^{X})\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!S\parallel X}(q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X})
and
((q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X}),q_{2}^{T})\in R
. From Definition
15
of parallel composition it follows that
q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}^{X}
. From the construction of
R^{\prime}
we confirm that
(q_{2}^{X},(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S}))\in R^{\prime}
.

•

i!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}
and
i!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}
. In this case, there are three possible options from Definition
18
.

–

q_{1}^{T}\xlongrightarrow{i!}{}^{\!\!T}q_{2}^{T}
and
q_{1}^{S}{\xlongrightarrow{i!}{}^{\!\!S}}q_{2}^{S}
.
Since
X
is an implementation and
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}
, it follows that
q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}^{X}
for some
q_{2}^{X}\in Q^{X}
(any implementation is a specification, see Definition
5
, which is input-enabled, see Definition
4
). Now, using Definition
15
of parallel composition it follows that
(q_{1}^{S},q_{1}^{X})\ {\xlongrightarrow{i!}{}^{\!\!S\parallel X}}(q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X})
. Using
R
and the third case of Definition
6
of refinement, it follows that
((q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X}),q_{2}^{T})\in R
. Thus from the construction of
R^{\prime}
we confirm that
(q_{2}^{X},(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S}))\in R^{\prime}
.

–

q_{1}^{S}{\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{i!}{}^{\!\!S}}
.
In this case,
(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S})=u
. Again, since
X
is an implementation and
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}
, it follows that
q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}^{X}
for some
q_{2}^{X}\in Q^{X}
. By construction of
R^{\prime}
it follows that
(q_{2}^{X},(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S}))=(q_{2}^{X},u)\in R^{\prime}
.

–

q_{1}^{T}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{i!}{}^{\!\!T}
and
q_{1}^{S}{\xlongrightarrow{i!}{}^{\!\!S}}q_{2}^{S}
.
Since
S\parallel X\leq T
holds and
(q_{1}^{X},(q_{1}^{T},q_{1}^{S}))\in R^{\prime}
implies, by construction
R^{\prime}
, that
((q_{1}^{S},q_{1}^{X}),q_{1}^{T})\in R
, we can conclude that
q_{1}^{T}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{i!}{}^{\!\!T}
implies
(q_{1}^{S},q_{1}^{X})\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{i!}{}^{\!\!S\parallel X}
from Definition
6
of refinement. Since
X
is an implementation and
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}
, it follows that
q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}
. Therefore, from Definition
15
of parallel composition it follows that
q_{1}^{S}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{i!}{}^{\!\!S}
. This contradicts with
q_{1}^{S}{\xlongrightarrow{i!}{}^{\!\!S}}q_{2}^{S}
, so this option is infeasible.

•

i!\notin\mathit{Act}^{T}
and
i!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}
. In this case, there are two possible options from Definition
18
.

–

q_{1}^{S}\xlongrightarrow{i!}{}^{\!\!S}q_{2}^{S}
and
q_{1}^{T}=q_{2}^{T}
.
Since
X
is an implementation and
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}
, it follows that
q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}^{X}
for some
q_{2}^{X}\in Q^{X}
(any implementation is a specification, see Definition
5
, which is input-enabled, see Definition
4
). Now, using Definition
15
of parallel composition it follows that
(q_{1}^{S},q_{1}^{X})\ {\xlongrightarrow{i!}{}^{\!\!S\parallel X}}(q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X})
. Using
R
and the forth case of Definition
6
of refinement, it follows that
((q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X}),q_{2}^{T})\in R
. Thus from the construction of
R^{\prime}
we confirm that
(q_{2}^{X},(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S}))\in R^{\prime}
.

–

q_{1}^{S}{\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{i!}{}^{\!\!S}}
.
In this case,
(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S})=u
. Again, since
X
is an implementation and
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}
, it follows that
q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}^{X}
for some
q_{2}^{X}\in Q^{X}
. By construction of
R^{\prime}
it follows that
(q_{2}^{X},(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S}))=(q_{2}^{X},u)\in R^{\prime}
.

So, in all feasible cases we can show that
q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}^{X}
and
(q_{2}^{X},(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S}))\in R^{\prime}
.

2.

(q_{1}^{T},q_{1}^{S})\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!T\backslash\!\backslash S}(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S})
for some
(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S})\in Q^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
and
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}
. By definition of
X
it follows that
\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}=\emptyset
, so this case can be ignored.

3.

q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}^{X}
for some
q_{2}^{X}\in Q^{X}
and
o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{X}\cap\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
. By definition of
X
it follows that
\mathit{Act}_{o}^{X}=\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
. Consider the following five possible cases from Definition
18
of the quotient that might result in
o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}(=\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cup\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T})
.

•

o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}
and
o?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}
. It follows from Definition
4
of a specification that
S
is input-enabled. Therefore, there is a transition
q_{1}^{S}\xlongrightarrow{o?}{}^{\!\!S}q_{2}^{S}
for some
q_{2}^{S}\in Q^{S}
. Now, from Definition
15
of parallel composition it follows that there is a transition
(q_{1}^{S},q_{1}^{X})\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!S\parallel X}(q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X})
. Using
R
and the third case of Definition
6
of refinement, it follows that
q_{1}^{T}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!T}q_{2}^{T}
and
((q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X}),q_{2}^{T})\in R
. Now, using Definition
18
of the quotient, it follows that
(q_{1}^{T},q_{1}^{S})\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!T\backslash\!\backslash S}(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S})
. And from the construction of
R^{\prime}
we confirm that
(q_{2}^{X},(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S}))\in R^{\prime}
.

•

o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}
and
o?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}
. This case is not feasible, as an action cannot be both an output and input in
T
.

•

o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}
and
o?\notin\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}
. In this case, it follows that
o\notin\mathit{Act}^{S}
at all. Then from Definition
15
it follows that there is a transition
(q_{1}^{S},q_{1}^{X})\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!S\parallel X}(q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X})
and
q_{1}^{S}=q_{2}^{S}
. Using
R
and the third case of Definition
6
of refinement, it follows that
q_{1}^{T}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!T}q_{2}^{T}
and
((q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X}),q_{2}^{T})\in R
. Now, using Definition
18
of the quotient, it follows that
(q_{1}^{T},q_{1}^{S})\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!T\backslash\!\backslash S}(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S})
. And from the construction of
R^{\prime}
we confirm that
(q_{2}^{X},(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S}))\in R^{\prime}
.

•

o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}\cap\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}
and
o?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}
. This case is not feasible, as an action cannot be both an output and input in
S
.

•

o!\notin\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}
and
o?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}
. It follows from Definition
4
of a specification that
S
is input-enabled. Therefore, there is a transition
q_{1}^{S}\xlongrightarrow{o?}{}^{\!\!S}q_{2}^{S}
for some
q_{2}^{S}\in Q^{S}
. Now, from Definition
15
of parallel composition it follows that there is a transition
(q_{1}^{S},q_{1}^{X})\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!S\parallel X}(q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X})

R

of refinement, it follows that
q_{1}^{T}=q_{2}^{T}
and
((q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X}),q_{2}^{T})\in R
. Now, using Definition

of the quotient, it follows that
(q_{1}^{T},q_{1}^{S})\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!T\backslash\!\backslash S}(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S})
. And from the construction of
R^{\prime}
we confirm that
(q_{2}^{X},(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S}))\in R^{\prime}
.

So, in all feasible cases we can show that
(q_{1}^{T},q_{1}^{S})\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!T\backslash\!\backslash S}(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S})
and
(q_{2}^{X},(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S}))\in R^{\prime}
.

4.

q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}^{X}

and

. By definition of
X
it follows that
\mathit{Act}_{o}^{X}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}=\emptyset
, so this case can be ignored.

5.

q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}^{X}
for some
q_{2}^{X}\in Q^{X}
and
d\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
. Consider two cases in
S
.

•

q_{1}^{S}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!S}
. In this case, there exists some

such that
q_{1}^{S}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!S}q_{2}^{S}
. Now, from Definition

of parallel composition it follows that there is a transition

. Using

and the fifth case of Definition
6
of refinement, it follows that
q_{1}^{T}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!T}q_{2}^{T}
and
((q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X}),q_{2}^{T})\in R
. Now, using Definition
18
of the quotient, it follows that

. And from the construction of
R^{\prime}
we confirm that
(q_{2}^{X},(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S}))\in R^{\prime}
.

•

q_{1}^{S}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!S}
. In this case, it follows from Definition
15
of parallel composition that there is no transition in

, i.e.,
(q_{1}^{S},q_{1}^{X})\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!S\parallel X}
. Furthermore, from Definition

. And from the construction of

.

So, in both cases we can show that
(q_{1}^{T},q_{1}^{S})\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!T\backslash\!\backslash S}(q_{2}^{X},q^{T\backslash\!\backslash S})
and
(q_{2}^{X},q^{T\backslash\!\backslash S})\in R^{\prime}
with
q^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}=(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S})
or
q^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}=u
.

So for all state pairs
(q_{1}^{X},(q_{1}^{T},q_{1}^{S}))\in R^{\prime}
we have shown that
R^{\prime}
witnesses the refinement
X\leq T\backslash\!\backslash S
. Now consider the five cases of Definition
6
for a state pair
(q_{1}^{X},u)\in R^{\prime}
.

1.

u\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!T\backslash\!\backslash S}u
for some
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}
. By definition of
X
it follows that
\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}=\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}
. Since
X
is an implementation and
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}
, it follows that
q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}^{X}
for some
q_{2}^{X}\in Q^{X}
(any implementation is a specification, see Definition
5
, which is input-enabled, see Definition
