). By construction of
R^{\prime}
it follows that
(q_{2}^{X},u)\in R^{\prime}
.

2.

u\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!T\backslash\!\backslash S}u
for some
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}
. By definition of
X
it follows that
\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}=\emptyset
, so this case can be ignored.

3.

q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}^{X}
for some
q_{2}^{X}\in Q^{X}
and
o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{X}\cap\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
. By definition of
X
it follows that
\mathit{Act}_{o}^{X}=\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
. From Definition
18
of the quotient it follows that
u\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!T\backslash\!\backslash S}u
. By construction of
R^{\prime}
it also follows that
(q_{2}^{X},u)\in R^{\prime}
.

4.

q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}^{X}
for some
q_{2}^{X}\in Q^{X}
and
o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{X}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
. By definition of
X
it follows that
\mathit{Act}_{o}^{X}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}=\emptyset
, so this case can be ignored.

5.

q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}^{X}
for some
q_{2}^{X}\in Q^{X}
and
d\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
. From Definition
18
of the quotient it follows that
u\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!T\backslash\!\backslash S}u
. By construction of
R^{\prime}
it also follows that
(q_{2}^{X},u)\in R^{\prime}
.

So for all state pairs
(q_{1}^{X},u)\in R^{\prime}
we have shown that
R^{\prime}
witnesses the refinement
X\leq T\backslash\!\backslash S
. Finally, since
R
witnesses
S\parallel X\leq T
it holds that
((q_{0}^{S},q_{o}^{X}),q_{0}^{T})\in R
(see Definition
6
). Thus by construction of
R^{\prime}
it holds that
(q_{0}^{X},(q_{0}^{T},q_{0}^{S}))\in R^{\prime}
. Therefore, we can now conclude that
R^{\prime}
witnesses
X\leq T\backslash\!\backslash X
.

(
S\parallel X\leq T\Leftarrow X\leq T\backslash\!\backslash S
) Since
X\leq T\backslash\!\backslash S
, it follows from Definition
6
of refinement that there exists a relation
R\in Q^{X}\times Q^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
that witness the refinement. Note that
Q^{S\parallel X}=Q^{S}\times Q^{X}
according to Definition
15
. Construct relation
R^{\prime}=\{((q_{1}^{S},q_{1}^{X}),q_{1}^{T})\in Q^{X}\times Q^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\mid(q_{1}^{X},(q_{1}^{T},q_{1}^{S}))\in R\}
. We will show that
R^{\prime}
witnesses
S\parallel X\leq T
. First consider the five cases of Definition
6
for a state pair
((q_{1}^{S},q_{1}^{X}),q_{1}^{T})\in R^{\prime}
.

1.

q_{1}^{T}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!T}q_{2}^{T}
for some
q_{2}^{T}\in Q^{T}
and
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S\parallel X}
. From Lemma
17
it follows that
\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}=\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S\parallel X}
. Consider the following five possible cases from Definition
15
of the parallel composition that might result in
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S\parallel X}(=\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{X}\cup\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S})
.

•

i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{X}
and
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}
. Since
S
and
X
are specifications and
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}
, it follows that
q_{1}^{S}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!S}q_{2}^{S}
for some
q_{2}^{S}\in Q^{S}
and
q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}^{X}
for some
q_{2}^{X}\in Q^{X}
(any specification is input-enabled, see Definition
4
). Therefore, using Definition
15
of parallel composition, it follows that
(q_{1}^{S},q_{1}^{X})\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!S\parallel X}(q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X})
. Also, using Definition
18
of the quotient it follows that
(q_{1}^{T},q_{1}^{S})\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!T\backslash\!\backslash S}(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S})
. Now, using
R
, the first case of Definition
6
of refinement, and
\mathit{Act}^{X}=\mathit{Act}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
by construction, it follows that
(q_{2}^{X},(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S}))\in R
. And from the construction of
R^{\prime}
we confirm that
((q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X}),q_{2}^{T})\in R^{\prime}
.

•

i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{X}
and
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}\cap\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}
. This case is infeasible, as an action cannot be both an output and input in
S
.

•

i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{X}
and
i?\notin\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}
. This case is infeasible, as
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{X}
and
i?\notin\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}
implies that
i\notin\mathit{Act}^{X}
, but from Definition
18
of the quotient it follows that
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}
implies that
i\in\mathit{Act}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}(=\mathit{Act}^{X})
.

•

i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{o}^{X}
and
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}
. This case is infeasible, as an action cannot be both an output and input in
X
.

•

i?\notin\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}
and
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}
. Since
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}
implies that
i!\notin\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}
, it follows that
i\notin\mathit{Act}^{S}
. From Definition
18
of quotient if follows that
(q_{1}^{T},q_{1}^{S})\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!T\backslash\!\backslash S}(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S})
and
q_{1}^{S}=q_{2}^{S}
. Now, using
R
, the first case of Definition
6
of refinement, and
\mathit{Act}^{X}=\mathit{Act}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
by construction, it follows that
q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}^{X}
and
(q_{2}^{X},(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S}))\in R
. Using Definition
15
of the parallel composition, it follows that
(q_{1}^{S},q_{1}^{X})\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!S\parallel X}(q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X})
. And from the construction of
R^{\prime}
we confirm that
((q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X}),q_{2}^{T})\in R^{\prime}
.

So, in all feasible cases we can show that
(q_{1}^{S},q_{1}^{X})\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!S\parallel X}(q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X})
and
((q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X}),q_{2}^{T})\in R^{\prime}
.

2.

q_{1}^{T}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!T}q_{2}^{T}
for some
q_{2}^{T}\in Q^{T}
and
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S\parallel X}
. From Lemma
17
it follows that
\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S\parallel X}=\emptyset
, so this case can be ignored.

3.

(q_{1}^{S},q_{1}^{X})\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!S\parallel X}(q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X})
for some
(q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X})\in Q^{S\parallel X}
and
o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S\parallel X}\cap\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}
. From Lemma
17
we have that
\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S\parallel X}=\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cup\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}\cup\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}
. Consider the following three cases that might result in
o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S\parallel X}
and
o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}
.

•

o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}
and
o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}
. In this case we have that
o?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
by Definition
18
, and thus by construction of
X
that
o?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}
. Now, using Definition
15
of the parallel composition, it follows that
q_{1}^{S}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!S}q_{2}^{S}
and
q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{o?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}^{X}
. Consider the following two cases for
T
.

–

q_{1}^{T}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!T}q_{2}^{T}
. In this case it follows that from Definition
18
of the quotient that
(q_{1}^{T},q_{1}^{S})\ {\xlongrightarrow{o?}{}^{\!\!T\backslash\!\backslash S}}\allowbreak(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S})
. Using
R
, the first case of Definition
6
of refinement, and
\mathit{Act}^{X}=\mathit{Act}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
by construction, it follows that
(q_{2}^{X},(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S}))\in R
. And from the construction of
R^{\prime}
we confirm that
((q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X}),q_{2}^{T})\in R^{\prime}
.

–

q_{1}^{T}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!T}
. In this case it follows from Definition
18
of the quotient that
(q_{1}^{T},q_{1}^{S})\xlongrightarrow{o?}{}^{\!\!T\backslash\!\backslash S}e
. By construction of
e
, it does not allow independent progress. But, since
X
is an implementation, all states in
X
allow independent progress, see Definition
5
14
14

14

This is the reason why
X
is assumed to be an implementation and not just a specification.
. Therefore, either
X
can delay indefinitely from state
q_{2}^{X}
or there exists a delay after which
X
can perform an output action. Neither of these options can be simulated by
T\backslash\!\backslash S
when in state
e
. Thus
(q_{2}^{X},e)\notin R
, i.e.,
X\nleq T\backslash\!\backslash S
. This contradicts with the assumption, thus this is not a feasible case.

•

o?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}
and
o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}
. In this case we have that
o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
by Definition
18
, and thus by construction of
X
that
o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{X}
. Now, using Definition
15
of the parallel composition, it follows that
q_{1}^{S}\xlongrightarrow{o?}{}^{\!\!S}q_{2}^{S}
and
q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}^{X}
. Using
R
, the third case of Definition
6
of refinement, and
\mathit{Act}^{X}=\mathit{Act}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
by construction, it follows that
(q_{1}^{T},q_{1}^{S})\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!T\backslash\!\backslash S}(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S})
and
(q_{2}^{X},(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S}))\in R
. Now, using Definition
18
of quotient again, it follows that
q_{1}^{T}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!T}q_{2}^{T}
. And from the construction of
R^{\prime}
we confirm that
((q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X}),q_{2}^{T})\in R^{\prime}
.

•

o\notin\mathit{Act}^{S}
and
o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}
. In this case we have that
o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
by Definition
18
, and thus by construction of
X
that
o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{X}
. Now, using Definition
15
of the parallel composition, it follows that
q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}^{X}
and
q_{1}^{S}=q_{2}^{S}
. Using
R
, the third case of Definition
6
of refinement, and
\mathit{Act}^{X}=\mathit{Act}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
by construction, it follows that
(q_{1}^{T},q_{1}^{S})\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!T\backslash\!\backslash S}(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S})
and
(q_{2}^{X},(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S}))\in R
. Now, using Definition
18
of quotient again, it follows that
q_{1}^{T}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!T}q_{2}^{T}
. And from the construction of
R^{\prime}
we confirm that
((q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X}),q_{2}^{T})\in R^{\prime}
.

So, in all feasible cases we can show that
q_{1}^{T}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!T}q_{2}^{T}
and
((q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X}),q_{2}^{T})\in R^{\prime}
.

4.

(q_{1}^{S},q_{1}^{X})\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!S\parallel X}(q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X})
for some
(q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X})\in Q^{S\parallel X}
and
o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S\parallel X}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}
. From Lemma
17
we have that
\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S\parallel X}=\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cup\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}\cup\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}
. So
\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S\parallel X}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}=(\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cup\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T})\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}=\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}\cup(\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T})\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}=\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}\cup\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}^{T}
. Consider the following five cases that might result in
o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S\parallel X}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}
.

•

o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}
and
o?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}^{T}
. This case is infeasible, as an action cannot be both an output and input in
S
.

•

o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}
and
o?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}^{T}
. This case is infeasible, as an action cannot be both an output and input in
S
.

•

o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}
and
o?\notin\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}
. In this case, we have that
o?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
from Definition
18
of the quotient. Therefore,
o?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}
by construction of
X
. Now, using Definition
15
of the parallel composition, it follows that
q_{1}^{S}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!S}q_{2}^{S}
and
q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{o?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}^{X}
. Since Definition
18
also requires that
\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}=\emptyset
, it follows that in this case
o\notin\mathit{Act}^{T}
. Thus, from Definition
18
it follows that
(q_{1}^{T},q_{1}^{S})\xlongrightarrow{o?}{}^{\!\!T\backslash\!\backslash S}(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S})
and
q_{1}^{T}=q_{2}^{T}
. Using
R
, the first case of Definition
6
of refinement, and
\mathit{Act}^{X}=\mathit{Act}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
by construction, it follows that
(q_{2}^{X},(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S}))\in R
. And from the construction of
R^{\prime}
we confirm that
((q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X}),q_{2}^{T})\in R^{\prime}
.

•

o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}
and
o?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}^{T}
. This case is infeasible, as an action cannot be both an output and input in
S
.

•

o!\notin\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}
and
o?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}^{T}
. In this case, we have that
o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
from Definition
18
of the quotient. Therefore,
o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{X}
by construction of
X
. Now, using Definition
15
of the parallel composition, it follows that
q_{1}^{S}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!S}q_{2}^{S}
and
q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{o?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}^{X}
. Using
R
, the fourth case of Definition
6
of refinement,
\mathit{Act}^{X}=\mathit{Act}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
by construction, and
o\notin\mathit{Act}^{T}
, it follows that
(q_{2}^{X},(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S}))\in R
and
q_{1}^{T}=q_{2}^{T}
. And from the construction of
R^{\prime}
we confirm that
((q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X}),q_{2}^{T})\in R^{\prime}
.

So, in all feasible cases we can show that
o\notin\mathit{Act}^{T}
,
q_{1}^{T}=q_{2}^{T}
, and
((q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X}),q_{2}^{T})\in R^{\prime}
.

5.

(q_{1}^{S},q_{1}^{X})\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!S\parallel X}(q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X})
for some
(q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X})\in Q^{S\parallel X}
and
d\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
. It follows from Definition
15
of the parallel composition that
q_{1}^{S}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!S}q_{2}^{S}
and
q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}^{X}
. Using
R
and the fifth case of Definition
6
of refinement it follows that
(q_{1}^{T},q_{1}^{S})\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!T\backslash\!\backslash S}q_{2}
for some
q_{2}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\in Q^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
and
(q_{2}^{X},q_{2}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}))\in R
. Now, by Definition
18
of the quotient it follows that
q_{1}^{T}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!T}q_{2}^{T}
(and
q_{1}^{S}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!S}q_{2}^{S}
). And from the construction of
R^{\prime}
we confirm that
((q_{2}^{S},q_{2}^{X}),q_{2}^{T})\in R^{\prime}
.

So for all state pairs
((q_{1}^{S},q_{1}^{X}),q_{1}^{T})\in R^{\prime}
we have shown that
R^{\prime}
witnesses the refinement
S\parallel X\leq T
. Finally, since
R
witnesses
X\leq T\backslash\!\backslash S
it holds that
(q_{0}^{X},(q_{0}^{T},q_{0}^{S}))\in R
(see Definition
6
). Thus by construction of
R^{\prime}
it holds that
((q_{0}^{S},q_{o}^{X}),q_{0}^{T})\in R^{\prime}
. Therefore, we can now conclude that
R^{\prime}
witnesses
S\parallel X\leq T
.

∎

Definition 21
.

Given a TIOTS
S=(Q,q_{0},\mathit{Act},\rightarrow)
and equivalence relation
\sim
on the set of states
Q
. The
\sim
-quotient
S
, denoted by
S/{\sim}
, is a specification
([Q]_{\sim},[q_{0}]_{\sim},\mathit{Act},{\rightarrow}/{\sim})
where
[Q]_{\sim}
is the set of all equivalence classes of
Q
15
15

15

Recall that an equivalent class is defined as
[q]_{\sim}=\{r\in Q\mid q\sim r\}
.
and
{\rightarrow}/{\sim}
being defined as
([q_{1}],a,[q_{2}])\in{\rightarrow}/{\sim}
if
(q_{1},a,q_{2})\in\rightarrow
for some
q_{1}\in[q_{1}]
and
q_{2}\in[q_{2}]
.

Lemma 18
.

Given specification automata
S=(\mathit{Loc}^{S},l_{0}^{S},\mathit{Act}^{S},\mathit{Clk}^{S},E^{S},\mathit{Inv}^{S})
and
T=(\mathit{Loc}^{T},l_{0}^{T},\allowbreak\mathit{Act}^{T},\mathit{Clk}^{T},E^{T},\allowbreak\mathit{Inv}^{T})
where
\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}=\emptyset
. Let
V_{0}=\{v\in[\mathit{Clk}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]\mid v(x_{\mathit{new}})=0\}
,
V_{>0}=[\mathit{Clk}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]\setminus V_{0}
, and
{\sim}=\{(q_{1},q_{2})\mid q_{1},q_{2}\in\{l_{e}\}\times V_{0}\}\cup\{(q,q)\mid q\in\{l_{e}\}\times V_{>0}\}\cup\{(q_{1},q_{2})\mid q_{1},q_{2}\in\{l_{u}\}\times[\mathit{Clk}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]\}\cup\{((l,v_{1}),(l,v_{2}))\mid l\in\mathit{Loc}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\setminus\{l_{e},l_{u}\},v_{1},v_{2}\in[\mathit{Clk}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}],\forall c\in\mathit{Clk}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\setminus\{x_{\mathit{new}}\},v_{1}(c)=v_{2}(c)\}
. Then
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\simeq\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}
.

Proof.

It follows directly from the definition of
\sim
that it is reflexive, symmetric, and transitive, thus it is an equivalence relation. Now, observe from Definition
21
that an equivalence quotient of a TIOTS does not alter the action set, i.e.,
\mathit{Act}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=\mathit{Act}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}}
. Let
R=\{(q,[q]_{\sim})\mid q\in Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\}
. We will show that
R
is a bisimulation relation. First, observe that
(q_{0},[q_{0}]_{\sim})\in R
. Consider a state pair
(q_{1},[r_{1}]_{\sim})\in R
. We have to check whether the six cases from Definition
20
of bisimulation hold.

1.

q_{1}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}q_{2}
,
q_{2}\in Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
, and
a\in\mathit{Act}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\cap\mathit{Act}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}}
. By the definition of an equivalence class and Definition
21
it follows immediately that
[q_{1}]_{\sim}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}}[q_{2}]_{\sim}
. By construction of
R
it follows that
(q_{2},[q_{2}]_{\sim})\in R
.

2.

q_{1}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}q_{2}
,
q_{2}\in Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
, and
a\in\mathit{Act}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\setminus\mathit{Act}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}}
. This case is infeasible, since
\mathit{Act}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=\mathit{Act}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}}
.

3.

[r_{1}]_{\sim}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}}[r_{2}]_{\sim}
,
[r_{2}]_{\sim}\in Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}}
, and
a\in\mathit{Act}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}}\cap\mathit{Act}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. By construction of
R
, we have to show that
\forall q_{1}\in[r_{1}]_{\sim}\exists q_{2}\in Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}:q_{1}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}q_{2}
,
q_{2}\in[r_{2}]_{\sim}
, and
(q_{2},[r_{2}]_{\sim})\in R
. Consider the following four cases based on the construction of
\sim
:

•

[r_{1}]_{\sim}=\{q\mid q\in\{l_{e}\}\times V_{0}\}
. In this case, let
q_{1}=(l_{e},v_{1})\in[r_{1}]_{\sim}
for some
v_{1}\in V_{0}
. From Definition
3
of the semantic of a TIOA it follows that
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
is in location
l_{e}
. From Definition
19
of the quotient it follows that the only possible transition in
T\backslash\!\backslash S
is
(l_{e},a,x_{\mathit{new}}=0,\emptyset,l_{e})
. Furthermore, since
[r_{1}]_{\sim}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}}[r_{2}]_{\sim}
, it holds that
\exists r_{1},r_{2}\in Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}:r_{1}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}r_{2}
. Following Definition
3
and the above observation, it holds that
r_{1}=(l_{e},v_{1}^{\prime})
and
r_{2}=(l_{e},v_{2}^{\prime})
for some
v_{1}^{\prime},v_{2}^{\prime}\in[\mathit{Clk}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]
,
v_{1}^{\prime}\models x_{\mathit{new}}=0
, and
v_{1}^{\prime}=v_{2}^{\prime}
. From
v_{1}^{\prime}\models x_{\mathit{new}}=0
it follows that
v_{1}^{\prime}(x_{\mathit{new}})=0
and
v_{1}^{\prime},v_{2}^{\prime}\in V_{0}
, and from
v_{1}^{\prime}=v_{2}^{\prime}
that
[r_{2}]_{\sim}=[r_{1}]_{\sim}
. Thus we can conclude that
q_{1}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}q_{2}
with
q_{2}\in[r_{2}]_{\sim}
. By construction of
R
it follows that
(q_{2},[r_{2}]_{\sim})\in R
.

•

[r_{1}]_{\sim}=\{q\mid q\in\{l_{e}\}\times V_{>0}\}
. This case is trivial, since
[r_{1}]_{\sim}=\{r_{1}\}=\{q_{1}\}
. Therefore, if
[r_{1}]_{\sim}\allowbreak{\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}}}[r_{2}]_{\sim}
,
\exists q_{2}\in[r_{2}]_{\sim}
such that
q_{1}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}q_{2}
.

•

[r_{1}]_{\sim}=\{q\mid q\in\{l_{u}\}\times[\mathit{Clk}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]\}
. In this case, let
q_{1}=(l_{u},v_{1})\in[r_{1}]_{\sim}
for some
v_{1}\in[\mathit{Clk}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]
. From Definition
3
of the semantic of a TIOA it follows that
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
is in location
l_{u}
. From Definition
19
of the quotient it follows that the only possible transition in
T\backslash\!\backslash S
is
(l_{u},a,\mathbf{T},\emptyset,l_{u})
. Furthermore, since
[r_{1}]_{\sim}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}}[r_{2}]_{\sim}
, it holds that
\exists r_{1},r_{2}\in Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}:r_{1}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}r_{2}
. Following Definition
3
and the above observation, it holds that
r_{1}=(l_{u},v_{1}^{\prime})
and
r_{2}=(l_{u},v_{2}^{\prime})
for some
v_{1}^{\prime},v_{2}^{\prime}\in[\mathit{Clk}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]
,
v_{1}^{\prime}\models\mathbf{T}
, and
v_{1}^{\prime}=v_{2}^{\prime}
. From
v_{1}^{\prime}=v_{2}^{\prime}
it follows that
[r_{2}]_{\sim}=[r_{1}]_{\sim}
. Thus we can conclude that
q_{1}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}q_{2}
with
q_{2}\in[r_{2}]_{\sim}
. By construction of
R
it follows that
(q_{2},[r_{2}]_{\sim})\in R
.

•

In this case, since
[r_{1}]_{\sim}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}}[r_{2}]_{\sim}
, it holds that
\exists r_{1},r_{2}\in Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}:r_{1}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}r_{2}
. Following Definition
3
of the semantic of a TIOA, it holds that
(l_{1},a,\varphi,c,l_{2})\in E^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
,
r_{1}=(l_{1},v_{1})
,
r_{2}=(l_{2},v_{2})
,
l_{1},l_{2}\in\mathit{Loc}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
,
v_{1},v_{2}\in[\mathit{Clk}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]
,
v_{1}\models\mathbf{\varphi}
,
v_{2}=v_{1}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}
, and
v_{2}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{2})
. From the construction of
\sim
, it follows that for any state
(l_{1}^{\prime},v_{1}^{\prime})\in[r_{1}]_{\sim}
it holds that
l_{1}^{\prime}=l_{1}
,
l_{1}\neq l_{e}
, and
\forall c\in\mathit{Clk}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\setminus\{x_{\mathit{new}}\}:v_{1}^{\prime}(c)=v_{1}(c)
. Since
x_{\mathit{new}}\notin\mathit{Clk}^{T}\cup\mathit{Clk}^{S}
and none of the possible rules for this location from Definition
19
of the quotient for TIOA use
x_{\mathit{new}}
in its guard, it follows that
v_{1}^{\prime}\models\varphi
. Furthermore, no matter whether
x_{\mathit{new}}\in c
or not, we have for
v_{2}^{\prime}=v_{1}^{\prime}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}
that
\forall c\in\mathit{Clk}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\setminus\{x_{\mathit{new}}\}:v_{2}^{\prime}(c)=v_{2}(c)
. Now consider the following three options for the target location
l_{2}
.

–

If
l_{2}=(l^{T},l^{S})
with
l^{T}\in\mathit{Loc}^{T}
and
l^{S}\in\mathit{Loc}^{S}
, then
\mathit{Inv}(l_{2})=\mathbf{T}
. Thus
v_{2}^{\prime}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{2})
.

–

If
l_{2}=l_{u}
, then
\mathit{Inv}(l_{2})=\mathbf{T}
. Thus
v_{2}^{\prime}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{2})
.

–

If
l_{2}=l_{e}
, then
\mathit{Inv}(l_{2})=x_{\mathit{new}}=0
. Also,
c=\{x_{\mathit{new}}\}
, thus
v_{2}(x_{\mathit{new}})=v_{2}^{\prime}(x_{\mathit{new}})=0
. Thus
v_{2}^{\prime}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{2})
.

Therefore, we can conclude that
(l_{1}^{\prime},v_{1}^{\prime})\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}(l_{2},v_{2}^{\prime})
,
(l_{2},v_{2}^{\prime})\in[r_{2}]_{\sim}
, and by construction of
R
that
((l_{2},v_{2}^{\prime}),[r_{2}]_{\sim})\in R
. Since we picked any state
(l_{1}^{\prime},v_{1}^{\prime})\in[r_{1}]_{\sim}
, the conclusion holds for all states
q_{1}\in[r_{1}]_{\sim}
.

4.

[r_{1}]_{\sim}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}}[r_{2}]_{\sim}
,
[r_{2}]_{\sim}\in Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}}
, and
a\in\mathit{Act}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}}\setminus\mathit{Act}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. This case is infeasible, since
\mathit{Act}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=\mathit{Act}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}}
.

5.

q_{1}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}q_{2}
,
q_{2}\in Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
, and
d\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
. By the definition of an equivalence class and Definition
21
it follows immediately that
[q_{1}]_{\sim}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}}[q_{2}]_{\sim}
. By construction of
R
it follows that
(q_{2},[q_{2}]_{\sim})\in R
.

6.

[r_{1}]_{\sim}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}}[r_{2}]_{\sim}
,
[r_{2}]_{\sim}\in Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}}
, and
d\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
. By construction of
R
, we have to show that
\forall q_{1}\in[r_{1}]_{\sim}\exists q_{2}\in Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}:q_{1}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}q_{2}
,
q_{2}\in[r_{2}]_{\sim}
, and
(q_{2},[r_{2}]_{\sim})\in R
. Consider the following three cases based on the construction of
\sim
:

•

[r_{1}]_{\sim}=\{q\mid q\in\{l_{e}\}\times V_{0}\}
. In this case, let
q_{1}=(l_{e},v_{1})\in[r_{1}]_{\sim}
for some
v_{1}\in V_{0}
. From Definition
3
of the semantic of a TIOA it follows that
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
is in location
l_{e}
. From Definition
19
of the quotient it follows that
\mathit{Inv}(l_{e})=x_{\mathit{new}}=0
. Furthermore, since
[r_{1}]_{\sim}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}}[r_{2}]_{\sim}
, it holds that
\exists r_{1},r_{2}\in Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}:r_{1}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}r_{2}
. Following Definition
3
and the above observation, it holds that
r_{1}=(l_{e},v_{1}^{\prime})
and
r_{2}=(l_{e},v_{2}^{\prime})
for some
v_{1}^{\prime},v_{2}^{\prime}\in[\mathit{Clk}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]
,
v_{2}^{\prime}=v_{1}^{\prime}+d
and
v_{2}^{\prime}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{e})
. From
v_{2}^{\prime}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{e})
it follows that
v_{2}^{\prime}(x_{\mathit{new}})=0
, thus
d=0
,
v_{1}^{\prime}=v_{2}^{\prime}
,
v_{1}^{\prime},v_{2}^{\prime}\in V_{0}
, and
[r_{2}]_{\sim}=[r_{1}]_{\sim}
. Thus we can conclude that
q_{1}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}q_{2}
with
q_{2}\in[r_{2}]_{\sim}
. By construction of
R
it follows that
(q_{2},[r_{2}]_{\sim})\in R
.

•

[r_{1}]_{\sim}=\{q\mid q\in\{l_{e}\}\times V_{>0}\}
. This case is trivial, since
[r_{1}]_{\sim}=\{r_{1}\}=\{q_{1}\}
. Therefore, if
[r_{1}]_{\sim}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}}[r_{2}]_{\sim}
,
\exists q_{2}\in[r_{2}]_{\sim}
such that
q_{1}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}q_{2}
.

•

[r_{1}]_{\sim}=\{q\mid q\in\{l_{u}\}\times[\mathit{Clk}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]\}
. In this case, let
q_{1}=(l_{u},v_{1})\in[r_{1}]_{\sim}
for some
v_{1}\in V_{0}
. From Definition
3
of the semantic of a TIOA it follows that
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
is in location
l_{u}
. From Definition
19
of the quotient it follows that
\mathit{Inv}(l_{u})=\mathbf{T}
. Furthermore, since
[r_{1}]_{\sim}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}}[r_{2}]_{\sim}
, it holds that
\exists r_{1},r_{2}\in Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}:r_{1}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}r_{2}
. Following Definition
3
and the above observation, it holds that
r_{1}=(l_{u},v_{1}^{\prime})
and
r_{2}=(l_{u},v_{2}^{\prime})
for some
v_{1}^{\prime},v_{2}^{\prime}\in[\mathit{Clk}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]
,
v_{2}^{\prime}=v_{1}^{\prime}+d
and
v_{2}^{\prime}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{u})
. Now it follows that
(l_{u},v_{2}^{\prime})\in[r_{1}]_{\sim}
, thus
[r_{2}]_{\sim}=[r_{1}]_{\sim}
. Therefore, we can conclude that
q_{1}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}q_{2}
with
q_{2}\in[r_{2}]_{\sim}
and by construction of
R
it follows that
(q_{2},[r_{2}]_{\sim})\in R
.

•

In this case, since
[r_{1}]_{\sim}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}}[r_{2}]_{\sim}
, it holds that
\exists r_{1},r_{2}\in Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}:r_{1}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}r_{2}
. Following Definition
3
of the semantic of a TIOA, it holds that
r_{1}=(l,v_{1})
,
r_{2}=(l,v_{2})
,
l\in\mathit{Loc}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
,
v_{1},v_{2}\in[\mathit{Clk}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]
,
v_{2}=v_{1}+d
,
v_{2}\models\mathit{Inv}(l)
, and
\forall d^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},d^{\prime}<d:v_{1}+d^{\prime}\models\mathit{Inv}(l)
.
From the construction of
\sim
, it follows that for any state
(l_{1}^{\prime},v_{1}^{\prime})\in[r_{1}]_{\sim}
it holds that
l_{1}^{\prime}=l_{1}
,
l_{1}\neq l_{e}
, and
\forall c\in\mathit{Clk}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\setminus\{x_{\mathit{new}}\}:v_{1}^{\prime}(c)=v_{1}(c)
. Therefore, we have for
v_{2}^{\prime}=v_{1}^{\prime}+d
that
\forall c\in\mathit{Clk}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\setminus\{x_{\mathit{new}}\}:v_{2}^{\prime}(c)=v_{2}(c)
; similarly, for
v_{1}^{\prime}+d^{\prime}
we have that
\forall c\in\mathit{Clk}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\setminus\{x_{\mathit{new}}\}:v_{1}^{\prime}+d^{\prime}(c)=v_{1}+d^{\prime}(c)
. From Definition
19
of the quotient for TIOA it follows that
\mathit{Inv}(l)=\mathit{Inv}(l^{\prime})=\mathbf{T}
. Thus
v_{2}^{\prime}\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{\prime})
and
v_{1}^{\prime}+d^{\prime}\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{\prime})
. Therefore, from Definition
3
again we have that
(l_{1}^{\prime},v_{1}^{\prime})\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}(l_{1}^{\prime},v_{2}^{\prime})
,
(l_{2},v_{2}^{\prime})\in[r_{2}]_{\sim}
, and by construction of
R
that
((l_{2},v_{2}^{\prime}),[r_{2}]_{\sim})\in R
. Since we picked any state
(l_{1}^{\prime},v_{1}^{\prime})\in[r_{1}]_{\sim}
, the conclusion holds for all states
q_{1}\in[r_{1}]_{\sim}
.

∎

The following definition defines the TIOTS of the
\sim
-quotient of
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
where all states consisting of the error location and a valuation where
u(x_{\mathit{new}})>0
are removed, as these states are never reachable.

Definition 22
.

Given specification automata
S=(\mathit{Loc}^{S},l_{0}^{S},\mathit{Act}^{S},\mathit{Clk}^{S},E^{S},\mathit{Inv}^{S})
and
T=(\mathit{Loc}^{T},l_{0}^{T},\allowbreak\mathit{Act}^{T},\mathit{Clk}^{T},E^{T},\allowbreak\mathit{Inv}^{T})
where
\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}=\emptyset
. Let
V_{0}=\{u\in[\mathit{Clk}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]\mid u(x_{\mathit{new}})=0\}
,
V_{>0}=[\mathit{Clk}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]\setminus V_{0}
, and
{\sim}=\{(q_{1},q_{2})\mid q_{1},q_{2}\in\{l_{e}\}\times V_{0}\}\cup\{(q,q)\mid q\in\{l_{e}\}\times V_{>0}\}\cup\{(q_{1},q_{2})\mid q_{1},q_{2}\in\{l_{u}\}\times[\mathit{Clk}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]\}\cup\{((l,v_{1}),(l,v_{2}))\mid l\in\mathit{Loc}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\setminus\{l_{e},l_{u}\},v_{1},v_{2}\in[\mathit{Clk}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}],\forall c\in\mathit{Clk}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\setminus\{x_{\mathit{new}}\},v_{1}(c)=v_{2}(c)\}
. The
reduced
\sim
-quotient of
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
, denoted by
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}
, is defined as TIOTS
(Q^{\rho},q_{0}^{\rho},\mathit{Act}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S},\rightarrow^{\rho})
where
Q^{\rho}=Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}}\setminus\{[q]\mid q\in\{l_{e}\}\times V_{>0}\}
,
q_{0}^{\rho}=q_{0}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}}
, and
\rightarrow^{\rho}=\rightarrow^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}}\cap\{(q_{1},a,q_{2})\mid q_{1},q_{2}\in Q,a\in\mathit{Act}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\}
.

Lemma 19
.

Given specification automata
S=(\mathit{Loc}^{S},l_{0}^{S},\mathit{Act}^{S},\mathit{Clk}^{S},E^{S},\mathit{Inv}^{S})
and
T=(\mathit{Loc}^{T},l_{0}^{T},\allowbreak\mathit{Act}^{T},\mathit{Clk}^{T},E^{T},\allowbreak\mathit{Inv}^{T})
where
\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}=\emptyset
. Then
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\simeq\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}
.

Proof.

Since bisimulation relation is an equivalence relation, it follows from Lemma
18
that it suffice to show that
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}\simeq\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}
. Let
R=\{(q,q)\mid q\in Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}\}
. We will show that
R
is a bisimulation relation. First, observe that
(q_{0},q_{0})\in R
by definition of
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}
. Instead of checking all six cases of bisimulation (Definition
20
), we will show that
q_{1}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}}q_{2}
for any
a\in\mathit{Act}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\cup\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
where
q_{1}\in Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}
and
q_{2}\in\{l_{e}\}\times V_{>0}
(i.e.,
q_{2}\notin Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}
). Only rules 5, 7, and 11 of Definition
19
of the quotient for TIOA have target location
l_{e}
, and thus could become
q_{2}
in the semantic of it. But notice that all three cases have clock reset
c=\{x_{\mathit{new}}\}
. Therefore, any state
(l_{e},u)
reached after taking a transition matching one of these three rules has a valuation
u(x_{\mathit{new}})=0
. Thus
(l_{e},u)\notin\{l_{e}\}\times V_{>0}
and
q_{1}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}}q_{2}
. Therefore, all reachable state pairs by bisimulation remains within
R
.

∎

Lemma 20
.

Given specification automata
S=(\mathit{Loc}^{S},l_{0}^{S},\mathit{Act}^{S},\mathit{Clk}^{S},E^{S},\mathit{Inv}^{S})
and
T=(\mathit{Loc}^{T},l_{0}^{T},\mathit{Act}^{T},\mathit{Clk}^{T},E^{T},\allowbreak\mathit{Inv}^{T})
where
\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}=\emptyset
. Let
f:Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}\rightarrow Q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
be defined as

•

f([((l^{T},l^{S}),v)]_{\sim})=((l^{T},v^{T}),(l^{S},v^{S}))
for any
v\in(\mathit{Clk}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0})
,
l^{T}\in\mathit{Loc}^{T}
,
v^{T}\in(\mathit{Clk}^{T}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0})
,
l^{S}\in\mathit{Loc}^{S}
, and
v^{S}\in(\mathit{Clk}^{S}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0})
such that
\forall x\in\mathit{Clk}^{T}:v(x)=v^{T}(x)
and
\forall x\in\mathit{Clk}^{S}:v(x)=v^{S}(x)
.

•

f([(l_{u},v)]_{\sim})=u
for any
v\in(\mathit{Clk}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0})
.

•

f([(l_{e},v)]_{\sim})=e
for any
v\in V_{0}
.

Then
f
is a bijective function.

Proof.

It follows directly from the definition that
f
is injective. We only have to show that
f
is surjective, where the last two cases are again trivial by definition of
f
. Thus we only have to show that any state
((l^{T},v^{T}),(l^{S},v^{S}))
maps to only a single state
[((l^{T},l^{S}),v)]_{\sim}
in
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}
. For this, note that
\sim
in Definition
22
contains
\{((l,v_{1}),(l,v_{2}))\mid l\in\mathit{Loc}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\setminus\{l_{e},l_{u}\},v_{1},v_{2}\in[\mathit{Clk}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}],\forall c\in\mathit{Clk}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\setminus\{x_{\mathit{new}}\},v_{1}(c)=v_{2}(c)\}
. Now we will show that state
((l^{T},v^{T}),(l^{S},v^{S}))
maps to only a single state
[((l^{T},l^{S}),v)]_{\sim}
using contradiction. Assume that state
((l^{T},v^{T}),(l^{S},v^{S}))
maps to two (or more) states
[((l_{1}^{T},l_{1}^{S}),v_{1})]_{\sim}
and
[((l_{2}^{T},l_{2}^{S}),v_{1})]_{\sim}
. From
\sim
it follows that either
l_{1}^{T}\neq l_{2}^{T}
,
l_{1}^{S}\neq l_{2}^{S}
, or
\exists c\in\mathit{Clk}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}\setminus\{x_{\mathit{new}}\}:v_{1}(c)\neq v_{2}(c)
. But since we only consider a single state
((l^{T},v^{T}),(l^{S},v^{S}))
, none of these options can hold. Thus our assumption does not hold, which concludes the proof.

∎

Since we now have a bijective function
f
relating states in
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}
and
\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
together, we can effectively relabel the states in
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}
from
[((l^{T},l^{S}),v)]_{\sim}
to
((l^{T},l^{S}),v^{T,S})
in all proofs below, where
v^{T,S}\in[\mathit{Clk}^{T}\cup\mathit{Clk}^{S}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]
with
\forall c\in\mathit{Clk}^{T}\cup\mathit{Clk}^{S}:v^{T,S}(c)=v(c)
. Notice that we remove the clock
x_{\mathit{new}}
from the state label, as this clock is not present in the state labels in
\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
. Thus
Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}=\{((l^{T},l^{S}),v)\mid l^{T}\in\mathit{Loc}^{T},l^{S}\in\mathit{Loc}^{S},v\in[\mathit{Clk}^{T}\cup\mathit{Clk}^{S}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]\}\cup\{u,e\}
.

Lemma 21
.

Given specification automata
S=(\mathit{Loc}^{S},l_{0}^{S},\mathit{Act}^{S},\mathit{Clk}^{S},E^{S},\mathit{Inv}^{S})
and
T=(\mathit{Loc}^{T},l_{0}^{T},\allowbreak\mathit{Act}^{T},\mathit{Clk}^{T},E^{T},\allowbreak\mathit{Inv}^{T})
where
\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}=\emptyset
. Then
\forall[q]\in Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}},\forall q\in[q]_{\sim}
:
q\in\mathrm{cons}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
iff
[q]\in\mathrm{cons}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}
.

Proof.

From Lemmas
18
and
19
it follows that
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\simeq\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}
. With
R_{1}=\{(q,[q]_{\sim})\mid q\in Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\}
being the bisimulation relation for
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\simeq\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}
and
R_{2}=\{(q,q)\mid q\in Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}\}
the bisimulation relation for
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}/{\sim}\simeq\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}
, we have that
R=\{(q,[q]_{\sim})\mid[q]_{\sim}\in Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}\}
is a bisimulation relation for
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\simeq\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}
. Using this bisimulation relation, we can easily see that
q
is an error state iff
[q]_{\sim}
is an error state.

We will now proof
q\in\mathrm{cons}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
iff
[q]\in\mathrm{cons}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}
by contradiction. First, assume that
[q]\in\mathrm{cons}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}
, but
\exists q^{\prime}\in[q]_{\sim}
such that
q^{\prime}\notin\mathrm{cons}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. That means that there exists a path from
q^{\prime}
to an error state
q^{\prime\prime}
. But since
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\simeq\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}
, it follows that
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}
can simulate the same path from
[q]_{\sim}
, and using
R
we have that
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}
reaches state
[q^{\prime\prime}]_{\sim}
. But since we assume that
[q]\in\mathrm{cons}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}
, it must hold that
[q^{\prime\prime}]_{\sim}
is not an error state. But this contradicts with the previous observation on error states. Showing the contradiction the other way around follows the same argument. Therefore, we can conclude that
q\in\mathrm{cons}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
iff
[q]\in\mathrm{cons}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}
.

∎

Lemma 22
.

Given specification automata
S=(\mathit{Loc}^{S},l_{0}^{S},\mathit{Act}^{S},\mathit{Clk}^{S},E^{S},\mathit{Inv}^{S})
and
T=(\mathit{Loc}^{T},l_{0}^{T},\allowbreak\mathit{Act}^{T},\mathit{Clk}^{T},E^{T},\allowbreak\mathit{Inv}^{T})
where
\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}=\emptyset
. Then
(\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}})^{\Delta}\simeq(\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho})^{\Delta}
.

Proof.

First, observe from Definition
12
that adversarial pruning does not alter the action set. Therefore, together with Definition
22
of the reduced quotient it follows that
(\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}})^{\Delta}
and
(\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho})^{\Delta}
have the same action set. From the proof of Lemma
21
it follows that
R=\{(q,[q]_{\sim})\mid q\in Q^{(\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}})^{\Delta}}\}
is a bisimulation relation showing
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\simeq\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}
. Finally, using the result of Lemma
21
that
\forall[q]\in Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}},\forall q\in[q]_{\sim}
:
q\in\mathrm{cons}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
iff
[q]\in\mathrm{cons}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}
together with Definition
12
, we can immediately conclude that
R=\{(q,[q]_{\sim})\mid q\in Q^{(\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}})^{\Delta}}\}
is also a bisimulation relation showing
(\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}})^{\Delta}\simeq(\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho})^{\Delta}
.

∎

Lemma 23
.

Given specification automata
S=(\mathit{Loc}^{S},l_{0}^{S},\mathit{Act}^{S},\mathit{Clk}^{S},E^{S},\mathit{Inv}^{S})
and
T=(\mathit{Loc}^{T},l_{0}^{T},\mathit{Act}^{T},\mathit{Clk}^{T},E^{T},\allowbreak\mathit{Inv}^{T})
where
\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}=\emptyset
. Then
\mathrm{imerr}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}\subseteq\mathrm{imerr}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
and
\mathrm{imerr}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\subseteq\mathrm{incons}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}
.

Proof.

First, observe that the semantic of a TIOA and the reduced quotient do not alter the action set. Therefore, it follows directly that
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}
and
\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
have the same action set and partitioning into input and output actions, except that
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}
has an additional input event
i_{\mathit{new}}
, i.e.,
\mathit{Act}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}\cup\{i_{\mathit{new}}\}=\mathit{Act}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
.

It follows from Lemma
20
that there is a bijective function
f
relating states from
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}
and
\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
together. Therefore, we can effectively say that they have the same state set (up to relabeling), i.e.,
Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}=Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. For brevity, in the rest of this proof we write we write
X=\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}
,
Y=\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
,
\mathit{Clk}=\mathit{Clk}^{T}\uplus\mathit{Clk}^{S}
, and
v^{S}
and
v^{T}
to indicate the part of a valuation
v
of only the clocks of
S
and
T
, respectively. Note that
x_{\mathit{new}}\notin\mathit{Clk}
, but
x_{\mathit{new}}\in\mathit{Clk}^{X}
.

\mathrm{imerr}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}\subseteq\mathrm{imerr}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. From Definition
19
of the quotient for TIOA and Definition
22
of the reduced
\sim
-quotient of
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
, it follows that states in
\{(l_{e},v)\in Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}\mid v(x_{\mathit{new}})=0\}=\mathrm{imerr}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}
are immediate error states, as only states with location
l_{e}
have an invariant other than
\mathbf{T}
. From Lemma
20
, we have that
\forall q\in f(q)=e
with
e\in Q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. From Definition
18
of the quotient for TIOTS, it follows immediately that
e
is an error state, since only
d=0
time delay is possible without any transition labeled with output actions. Thus
e\in\mathrm{imerr}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. This shows that
\mathrm{imerr}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}\subseteq\mathrm{imerr}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
.

\mathrm{imerr}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\subseteq\mathrm{incons}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}
. From Definition
18
of the quotient for TIOTS, it follows that state
e
is an immediate error state and that states in
\{(q^{T},q^{S})\in Q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\mid q^{T}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\wedge q^{S}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\}
are potentially error states, as these states have no outgoing delay transition, i.e.,
(q^{T},q^{S})\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. Some states of this set are actual immediate error states if
\nexists o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
s.t.
(q^{T},q^{S})\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. By Definition
18
we have that
\mathit{Act}_{o}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cup\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}
. Consider the following two cases.

•

o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}
. Assume that
(q^{T},q^{S})\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
, such that
(q^{T},q^{S})
is actually an error state. It follows from Definition
3
of the semantic that
q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l^{T},v^{T})
and
v^{T}+d\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{T})
; similarly we have that
q^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l^{S},v^{S})
and
v^{S}+d\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
. Since TIOTSs are time additive, see Definition
1
, we can assume that for
\forall d^{\prime}<d:v^{T}+d^{\prime}\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{T})
16
16

16

In case there would be a
d^{\prime}<d
such that
v^{T}+d^{\prime}\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{T})
, we can first delay
d^{\prime}
in
\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
such that the reached state can no longer delay.
. Thus
v^{T}+0\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{T})
, which simplifies to
v^{T}\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{T})
. Again, using time additivity of TIOTS and
v^{S}+d\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
, we have that
v^{S}+0\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
. Combining this information, we have that
v\models\neg\mathit{Inv}(l^{T})\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
, where we used the fact that
\mathit{Clk}^{T}\cap\mathit{Clk}^{S}=\emptyset
. Now, using Definition
19
of the quotient for TIOA and Definition
3
of the semantics, we have that
(l^{T},l^{S},v)\xlongrightarrow{i_{\mathit{new}}}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}(l_{e},v)
. Since the target state
(l_{e},v)
is an immediate error state and
i_{\mathit{new}}
is an input action, it follows the controllable predecessor operator that
(l^{T},l^{S},v)\in\mathrm{incons}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}
.

•

o?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}
. Since
S
is a specification, it is input-enabled, see Definition
4
. Therefore,
q^{S}\xlongrightarrow{o?}{}^{\!\!\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. From the second rule of Definition
18
of the quotient for TIOTS, it follows that
(q^{T},q^{S})\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. Therefore, in this case state
(q^{T},q^{S})
is not an error state in
\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
.

∎

Lemma 24
.

Given specification automata
S=(\mathit{Loc}^{S},l_{0}^{S},\mathit{Act}^{S},\mathit{Clk}^{S},E^{S},\mathit{Inv}^{S})
and
T=(\mathit{Loc}^{T},l_{0}^{T},\mathit{Act}^{T},\mathit{Clk}^{T},E^{T},\allowbreak\mathit{Inv}^{T})
where
\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}=\emptyset
. Denote
X=\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}
and
Y=\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
, and let
d\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
and
q_{1},q_{2}\in Q^{X}\cap Q^{Y}
with
q_{1}=(l^{T},l^{S},v)
for some
v\in(\mathit{Clk}^{T}\uplus\mathit{Clk}^{S}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0})
. If
v\not\models\neg\mathit{Inv}(l^{T})\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
, then
q_{1}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}
if and only if
q_{1}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{2}
.

Proof.

It follows from Lemma
20
that there is a bijective function
f
relating states from
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}
and
\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
together. Therefore, we can effectively say that they have the same state set (up to relabeling), i.e.,
Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}=Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. For brevity, in the rest of this proof we write we write
\mathit{Clk}=\mathit{Clk}^{T}\uplus\mathit{Clk}^{S}
, and
v^{S}
and
v^{T}
to indicate the part of a valuation
v
of only the clocks of
S
and
T
, respectively. Note that
x_{\mathit{new}}\notin\mathit{Clk}
, but
x_{\mathit{new}}\in\mathit{Clk}^{X}
.

From Definition
19
of the quotient for TIOA it follows that
\mathit{Inv}((l^{T},l^{S}))=\mathbf{T}
. Therefore, with Definition
3
of the semantic and Definition
22
of the
\sim
-reduced quotient of
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
it follows that
q_{1}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}
is possible for any
d\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
and any valuation
v
. Thus
q_{1}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{2}
implies
q_{1}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}
.

It remains to show the other way around. Observe from Definition
18
of the quotient for TIOTS that there are two cases involving a delay (actually three, but we do not consider the universal location in this lemma). So a delay is only possible from
q_{1}
if either
q_{1}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}q_{2}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\wedge q_{1}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}q_{2}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
or
q_{1}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. So a delay is
not
possible if
q_{1}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\wedge q_{1}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}q_{2}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. It follows from Definition
3
of the semantic that
q_{1}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l^{T},v^{T})
and
v^{T}+d\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{T})
or
\exists d^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},d^{\prime}<d:v^{T}+d^{\prime}\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{T})
; similarly we have that
q_{1}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l^{S},v^{S})
,
v^{S}+d\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
, and
\forall d^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},d^{\prime}<d:v^{S}+d^{\prime}\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
. Without loss of generality, we can state that
d^{\prime}=0
17
17

17

In case there would be a
d^{\prime}>0
such that
v^{T}+d^{\prime}\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{T})
, we can first delay
d^{\prime}
in
\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
such that the reached state can no longer delay.
, so
v^{T}+0\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{T})
, which simplifies to
v^{T}\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{T})
. We have also that
v^{S}+0\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
. Combining this information, we have that
v\models\neg\mathit{Inv}(l^{T})\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
, where we used the fact that
\mathit{Clk}^{T}\cap\mathit{Clk}^{S}=\emptyset
. But this contradicts with the assumption in the lemma. Thus we can conclude that if
v\not\models\neg\mathit{Inv}(l^{T})\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
, then
q_{1}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}
implies
q_{1}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{2}
.

∎

Lemma 25
.

Given specification automata
S=(\mathit{Loc}^{S},l_{0}^{S},\mathit{Act}^{S},\mathit{Clk}^{S},E^{S},\mathit{Inv}^{S})
and
T=(\mathit{Loc}^{T},l_{0}^{T},\mathit{Act}^{T},\mathit{Clk}^{T},E^{T},\allowbreak\mathit{Inv}^{T})
where
\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}=\emptyset
. Then
\mathrm{cons}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}=\mathrm{cons}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
.

Proof.

We will proof this by using the
\Theta
operator. First, observe that the semantic of a TIOA and the reduced quotient do not alter the action set. Therefore, it follows directly that
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}
and
\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
have the same action set and partitioning into input and output actions, except that
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}
has an additional input event
i_{\mathit{new}}
, i.e.,
\mathit{Act}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}\cup\{i_{\mathit{new}}\}=\mathit{Act}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
.

It follows from Lemma
20
that there is a bijective function
f
relating states from
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}
and
\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
together. Therefore, we can effectively say that they have the same state set (up to relabeling), i.e.,
Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}=Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. For brevity, in the rest of this proof we write we write
X=\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}
,
Y=\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
,
\mathit{Clk}=\mathit{Clk}^{T}\uplus\mathit{Clk}^{S}
, and
v^{S}
and
v^{T}
to indicate the part of a valuation
v
of only the clocks of
S
and
T
, respectively. Note that
x_{\mathit{new}}\notin\mathit{Clk}
, but
x_{\mathit{new}}\in\mathit{Clk}^{X}
.

We will show for any postfixed point
P
of
\Theta
that
\Theta^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}(P)\subseteq\Theta^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}(P)
and
\Theta^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}(P)\subseteq\Theta^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}(P)
.

(
\Theta^{X}(P)\subseteq\Theta^{Y}(P)
) Consider a state
q^{X}\in P
. Because
P
is a postfixed point of
\Theta^{X}
, it follows that
q^{X}\in\Theta^{X}(P)
. From the definition of
\Theta
, it follows that
q^{X}\in\overline{\mathrm{err}^{X}(\overline{P})}
and
q^{X}\in\{q_{1}\in Q^{X}\mid\forall d\geq 0:[\forall q_{2}\in Q^{X}:q_{1}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}\Rightarrow q_{2}\in P\wedge\forall i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}:\exists q_{3}\in P:q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}]\vee[\exists d^{\prime}\leq d\wedge\exists q_{2},q_{3}\in P\wedge\exists o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{X}:q_{1}\xlongrightarrow{d^{\prime}}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}\wedge q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}\wedge\forall i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}:\exists q_{4}\in P:q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{4}]\}
. We will focus on the second part of the definition of
\Theta
.

Consider a
d\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
. Then the left-hand side or the right-hand side of the disjunction is true (or both).

•

Assume the left-hand side is true, i.e.,
\forall q_{2}\in Q^{X}:q^{X}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}\Rightarrow q_{2}\in P\wedge\forall i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}:\exists q_{3}\in P:q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}
. Pick a
q_{2}\in Q^{X}
. The implication is true when
q^{X}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}
or
q^{X}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}\wedge q_{2}\in P\wedge\forall i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}:\exists q_{3}\in P:q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}
.

–

Consider the first case. This case is only applicable if
q^{X}=(l_{e},v)
, since in Definition
19
of the quotient for TIOA only location
l_{e}
has an invariant other than
T
. But then
q^{X}\in\mathrm{imerr}^{X}
. This contradicts with the fact that
q^{X}\in\Theta(P)
implies that
q^{X}\in\overline{\mathrm{err}^{X}(\overline{P})}
. Thus this case is infeasible.

–

Consider the second case. From Definition
3
of the semantic of a TIOA and Definition
22
of the
\sim
-reduced quotient of
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
it follows that
v_{1}+d\models\mathit{Inv}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}(l_{1})
for
q^{X}=(l_{1},v_{1})
,
q_{2}=(l_{1},v_{1}+d)
,
l_{1}\in\mathit{Loc}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
, and
v_{1}\in[\mathit{Clk}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]
. Since
q^{X}\in\overline{\mathrm{err}^{X}(\overline{P})}
, we have that
l_{1}\neq l_{e}
, thus
\mathit{Inv}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}(l_{1})=\mathbf{T}
. Now, pick
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}
and
q_{3}\in Q^{X}
such that
q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}
and
q_{3}\in P
. From Definition
3
of the semantic of a TIOA it follows that
(l_{1},i?,\varphi,c,l_{3})\in E^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
,
q_{3}=(l_{3},v_{3})
,
v_{1}+d\models\varphi
,
v_{3}=v_{1}+d[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}
, and
v_{3}\models\mathit{Inv}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}(l_{3})
.

From Lemma
24
it follows that
q^{X}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{2}
if
v\not\models\neg\mathit{Inv}(l^{T})\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
. In case that
v\models\neg\mathit{Inv}(l^{T})\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
, we have from Definitions
19
,
3
, and
22
that
q^{X}\xlongrightarrow{i_{\mathit{new}}}{}^{\!\!X}e
. But since
e\in\mathrm{err}^{X}(\overline{P})
, it follows that
e\notin P
. Therefore, this case is infeasible. Thus we have that
q^{X}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{2}
in
Y
.

Now, consider the ten cases from Definition
19
of quotient of TIOAs. Remember that
\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}=\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}=\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}\cup\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cup\{i_{\mathit{new}}\}
.

1.

i?\in\mathit{Act}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}^{T}
,
l_{1}=(l_{1}^{T},l_{1}^{S})
,
l_{3}=(l_{3}^{T},l_{3}^{S})
,
\varphi=\varphi^{T}\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{3}^{T})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{T}}\wedge\varphi^{S}\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{3}^{S})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}}
,
c=c^{T}\cup c^{S}
,
(l_{1}^{T},i,\varphi^{T},c^{T},l_{3}^{T})\in E^{T}
, and
(l_{1}^{S},i,\varphi^{S},c^{S},l_{3}^{S})\in E^{S}
. Since
v_{1}+d\models\varphi
, it holds that
v_{1}+d\models\varphi^{T}
,
v_{1}+d\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{3}^{T})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{T}}
,
v_{1}+d\models\varphi^{S}
,
v_{1}+d\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
, and
v_{1}+d\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{3}^{S})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}}
. Because
\mathit{Clk}^{S}\cap\mathit{Clk}^{T}=\emptyset
, it holds that
v_{1}^{T}+d\models\varphi^{T}
,
v_{1}^{T}+d\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{3}^{T})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{T}}
,
v_{1}^{S}+d\models\varphi^{S}
,
v_{1}^{S}+d\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
, and
v_{1}^{S}+d\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{3}^{S})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}}
. Since
v_{3}=v_{1}+d[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}
, it holds that
v_{3}^{T}=v_{1}^{T}+d[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{T}}
and
v_{3}^{S}=v_{1}^{S}+d[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}}
. Therefore,
v_{3}^{T}+d\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{3}^{T})
and
v_{3}^{S}+d\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{3}^{S})
.

Combining all information about
T
, we have that
(l_{1}^{T},i,\varphi^{T},c^{T},l_{2}^{T})\in E^{T}
,
v_{1}^{T}+d\models\varphi^{T}
,
v_{3}^{T}=v_{1}^{T}+d[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{T}}
, and
v_{3}^{T}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{3}^{T})
. Therefore, from Definition
3
it follows that
(l_{1}^{T},v_{1}^{T}+d)\xlongrightarrow{i}(l_{3}^{T},v_{3}^{T})
in
\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
. Combining all information about
S
, we have that
(l_{1}^{S},i,\varphi^{S},c^{S},l_{2}^{S})\in E^{S}
,
v_{1}^{S}+d\models\varphi^{S}
,
v_{3}^{S}=v_{1}^{S}+d[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}}
, and
v_{3}^{S}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{3}^{S})
. Therefore, from Definition
3
it follows that
(l_{1}^{S},v_{1}^{S}+d)\xlongrightarrow{i}(l_{3}^{S},v_{3}^{S})
in
\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
.

Now, from Definition
18
it follows that
((l_{1}^{T},v_{1}^{T}+d),(l_{1}^{S},v_{1}^{S}+d))=(l_{1}^{T},l_{1}^{S},v_{1}+d)=q_{2}^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!Y}((l_{3}^{T},v_{3}^{T}),\allowbreak(l_{3}^{S},v_{3}^{S}))=(l_{3}^{T},l_{3}^{S},v_{3})=q_{3}^{Y}
in
Y
. Thus, we can simulate a transition in
Y
. Also, observe now that
q_{2}=q_{2}^{Y}
and
q_{3}=q_{3}^{Y}
.

2.

i?\in\mathit{Act}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}^{T}
,
l_{1}=(l^{T},l_{1}^{S})
,
l_{3}=(l^{T},l_{3}^{S})
,
\varphi=\varphi^{S}\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{3}^{S})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}}
,
c=c^{S}
,
l^{T}\in\mathit{Loc}^{T}
, and
(l_{1}^{S},i!,\varphi^{S},c^{S},l_{3}^{S})\in E^{S}
. Since
v_{1}+d\models\varphi
and
\mathit{Clk}^{S}\cap\mathit{Clk}^{T}=\emptyset
, it holds that
v_{1}^{S}+d\models\varphi^{S}
,
v_{1}^{S}+d\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
, and
v_{1}^{S}+d\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{3}^{S})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}}
. Since
v_{3}=v_{1}+d[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}
and
c=c^{S}
, it holds that
v_{3}^{S}=v_{1}^{S}+d[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}}
,
v_{3}^{T}=v_{1}^{T}+d
, and
v_{3}^{S}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{3}^{S})
. Combining all information above about
S
, it follows from Definition
3
that
(l_{1}^{S},v_{1}^{S}+d)\xlongrightarrow{i!}(l_{3}^{S},v_{3}^{S})
in
\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
. From Definition
3
it also follows that
(l^{T},v_{1}^{T}+d)\in Q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. Therefore, following Definition
18
it follows that
((l^{T},v_{1}^{T}+d),(l_{1}^{S},v_{1}^{S}+d))=(l^{T},l_{1}^{S},v_{1}+d)=q_{2}^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{i!}{}^{\!\!Y}((l^{T},v_{1}^{T}+d),(l_{3}^{S},v_{3}^{S}))=(l^{T},l_{3}^{S},v_{3})=q_{3}^{Y}
in
Y
. Thus, we can simulate a transition in
Y
. Also, observe now that
q_{2}=q_{2}^{Y}
and
q_{3}=q_{3}^{Y}
.

3.

i!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}
,
l_{1}=(l^{T},l_{1}^{S})
,
l_{3}=l_{u}
,
\varphi=\neg G_{S}
,
c=\emptyset
,
l^{T}\in\mathit{Loc}^{T}
and
G_{S}=\bigvee\{\varphi^{S}\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{3}^{S})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}}\mid(l_{1}^{S},a,\varphi^{S},c^{S},l_{3}^{S})\in E^{S}\}
. Since
v_{1}+d\models\varphi
and
\mathit{Clk}^{S}\cap\mathit{Clk}^{T}=\emptyset
, it holds that
v_{1}^{S}+d\models\neg G_{S}
. Therefore,
v_{1}^{S}+d\not\models G_{S}
, which indicates that
\forall(l_{1}^{S},a,\varphi^{S},c^{S},l_{3}^{S})\in E^{S}
:
v_{1}^{S}+d\not\models\varphi^{S}\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{3}^{S})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}}
. This means that
v_{1}^{S}+d\not\models\varphi^{S}
or
v_{1}^{S}+d\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{3}^{S})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}}
or both, where the second option is equivalent to
v_{1}^{S}+d[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}}\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{3}^{S})
. Following Definition
3
, we can conclude that
(l_{1}^{S},v_{1}^{S}+d)\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{\ a\ }
in
\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
. From Definition
3
it also follows that
(l^{T},v_{1}^{T}+d)\in Q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. Now, following Definition
18
, we have transition
((l^{T},v_{1}^{T}+d),(l_{1}^{S},v_{1}^{S}+d))=(l^{T},l_{1}^{S},v_{1}+d)=q_{2}^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!Y}u=q_{3}^{Y}
in
Y
. Thus we can simulate a transition in
Y
. Also, observe now that
q_{2}=q_{2}^{Y}
and
q_{3}=q_{3}^{Y}
(where
(l_{u},v_{3})
is mapped into
u
by
f
from Lemma
20
).

4.

i?\in\mathit{Act}^{S}\cup\mathit{Act}^{T}
,
l_{1}=(l^{T},l^{S})
,
l_{3}=l_{u}
,
\varphi=\neg\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
,
c=\emptyset
,
l^{T}\in\mathit{Loc}^{T}
, and
l^{S}\in\mathit{Loc}^{S}
.(If
i?=i_{\mathit{new}}
, this case is trivial, see item 8 and 10 below.) Since
v_{1}+d\models\varphi
and
\mathit{Clk}^{S}\cap\mathit{Clk}^{T}=\emptyset
, it holds that
v_{1}^{S}+d\models\neg\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
. Therefore,
v_{1}^{S}+d\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
. Since we delayed into state
q_{2}^{Y}
, it must hold that the delay was according to rule 6 of Definition
18
of the quotient for TIOTS. Therefore,
q_{2}^{Y}=u\in P
. From Definition
18
it also follows that
u=q_{2}^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!Y}u=q_{3}^{Y}
in
Y
. Thus we can simulate a transition in
Y
. Also, observe now that
q_{3}=q_{3}^{Y}
(where
(l_{u},v_{3})
is mapped into
u
by
f
from Lemma
20
).

5.

i!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}
,
l_{1}=(l_{1}^{T},l_{1}^{S})
,
l_{3}=l_{e}
,
\varphi=\varphi^{S}\wedge\mathit{Inv}{l_{1}^{S}}\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{3}^{S})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}}\wedge\neg G_{T}
,
c=\{x_{\mathit{new}}\}
,
(l_{1}^{S},a,\varphi^{S},c^{S},l_{3}^{S})\in E^{S}
, and
G_{T}=\bigvee\{\varphi^{T}\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{3}^{T})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{T}}\mid(l_{1}^{T},a,\varphi^{T},c^{T},l_{3}^{T})\in E^{T}\}
. Since the target location is the error location, it holds that
q_{3}\notin P
. Thus this case is not feasible.

6.

a=i_{\mathit{new}}
,
l_{1}=(l^{T},l^{S})
,
l_{3}=l_{e}
,
\varphi=\neg\mathit{Inv}(l^{T})\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
,
c=\{x_{\mathit{new}}\}
,
l^{T}\in\mathit{Loc}^{T}
, and
q^{S}\in\mathit{Loc}^{S}
. Since the target location is the error location, it holds that
q_{3}\notin P
. Thus this case is not feasible.

7.

a=i_{\mathit{new}}
,
l_{1}=l_{3}=(l_{1}^{T},l_{1}^{S})
,
\varphi=\mathit{Inv}(l^{T})\vee\neg\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
and
c=\emptyset
. First note that
i_{\mathit{new}}\notin\mathit{Act}^{Y}
. Now, since
c=\emptyset
, it follows that
v_{3}=v_{1}+d
. Therefore,
q_{2}=q_{3}
. Since
q_{3}\in P
, it follows
q_{2}\in P
. Since
q_{2}=q_{2}^{Y}
, it follows that
q_{2}^{Y}\in P
.

8.

i?\in\mathit{Act}^{T}\setminus\mathit{Act}^{S}
,
l_{1}=(l_{1}^{T},l^{S})
,
l_{3}=(l_{3}^{T},l^{S})
,
\varphi=\varphi^{T}\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{3}^{T})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{T}}\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
,
c=c^{T}
,
l^{S}\in\mathit{Loc}^{S}

in

. Thus, we can simulate a transition in

. Also, observe now that

and

.

q_{2}

Assume the right-hand side is true, i.e.,

Now consider the output transition labeled with

{q^{X}\xlongrightarrow{d^{\prime}}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{2}}\wedge q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{3}

(
