. Using Definition
22
of the reduced
\sim
-quotient of
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
and Lemma
20
, we can rearrange the states into
((l_{2}^{T},l^{S}),v_{2})=((l_{2}^{T},v_{2}^{T}),(l^{S},v_{2}^{S}))=q_{2}^{Y}
and
((l_{3}^{T},l^{S}),v_{3})=((l_{3}^{T},v_{3}^{T}),(l^{S},v_{3}^{S}))=q_{3}^{Y}
, and we can show that
q_{2}^{Y}\xrightarrow{i?}q_{3}^{Y}
is a transition in
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}=X
. Also, observe now that
q_{2}^{X}=q_{2}^{Y}
and
q_{3}^{X}=q_{3}^{Y}
.

*

v_{2}^{S}\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{2}^{S})
. In this case, state
q_{2}=(l_{2}^{T},v_{2}^{T},l_{2}^{S},v_{2}^{S})
cannot be reached by delaying into it, since
v_{2}^{S}\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{2}^{S})
implies with Definition
3
of the semantic that
\forall q^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\in Q^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
we have
q^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}q_{2}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. From Definition
18
we have that in this case
q^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!Y}u
, and
q_{2}^{Y}\neq u
. Thus this case is infeasible.

4.

d\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
,
q_{2}^{Y}=(q_{2}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}},q_{2}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}})
,
q_{3}^{Y}=(q_{3}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}},q_{3}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}})
,
q_{2}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}q_{3}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
, and
q_{2}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\allowbreak{\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}}q_{3}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. This case is infeasible, since
i?\neq d
.

5.

i!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}
,
q_{2}^{Y}=(q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}},q^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}})
,
q_{3}^{Y}=u
,
q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\in Q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
, and
q^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{i!}{}^{\!\!\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. From Definition
3
of semantic it follows that
q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l^{T},v^{T})
and
q^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l^{S},v^{S})
. There are two reasons why
q^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{i!}{}^{\!\!\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
: there might be no edge in
E^{S}
labeled with action
i!
from location
l^{S}
or none of the edges labeled with
i!
from
l^{S}
are enabled. An edge
(l^{S},i!,\varphi,c,l^{S\prime})\in E^{S}
is not enabled if
v^{S}\not\models\varphi
or
v^{S}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{S\prime})
(or both), which can also be written as
v^{S}\not\models\varphi\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l^{S\prime})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}
. Looking at the third rule in Definition
19
of the quotient for TIOA, we have that
((l^{T},l^{S}),i?,\neg G_{S},\emptyset,l_{u})\in E^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
and
v^{S}\not\models G_{S}
, or
v^{S}\models\neg G_{S}
. Because
\mathit{Clk}^{T}\cap\mathit{Clk}^{S}=\emptyset
, it holds that
v\models\neg G_{S}
.

Now, since
\mathit{Inv}(l_{u})=\textbf{T}
and no clocks are reset, it holds that
v[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in\emptyset}=v\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{u})
. From Definition
3
it now follows that
((l^{T},l^{S}),v)\xrightarrow{i?}(l_{u},v_{3})
is a transition in
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
. From the state label renaming function
f
from Lemma
20
we have that
q_{3}^{X}=f((l_{u},v_{3}))=u=q_{3}^{Y}
and
q_{2}^{X}=q_{2}^{Y}
. And from Definition
22
of the reduced
\sim
-quotient of
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
we have that
q_{2}^{Y}\xrightarrow{i?}q_{3}^{Y}
is a transition in
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}=X
.

6.

d\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
,
q_{2}^{Y}=(q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}},q^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}})
,
q_{3}^{Y}=u
,
q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\in Q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
, and
q^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. This case is infeasible, since
i?\neq d
.

7.

i!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}
,
q_{2}^{Y}=(q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}},q^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}})
,
q_{3}^{Y}=e
,
q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
, and
q^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. Since the target location is the error location, it holds that
q_{3}\notin P
. Thus this case is not feasible.

8.

i\in\mathit{Act}^{T}\cup\mathit{Act}^{S}\cup\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
,
q_{2}^{Y}=u
,
q_{3}^{Y}=u
. There are two cases how
q_{2}^{Y}=u
could have been reached by a delay.

*

q^{Y}=u
. In this case, it follows directly from Definition
19
that
(l_{u},i?,\mathbf{T},\emptyset,l_{u})\in E^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
. Since any valuation satisfies a true guard and by definition of
T\backslash\!\backslash S
that
\mathit{Inv}(l_{u})=\mathbf{T}
, we have with Definition
3
of semantic that
(l_{u},v)\xrightarrow{i?}(l_{u},v)
is a transition in
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
. From the state label renaming function
f
from Lemma
20
we have that
q_{2}^{X}=q_{2}^{Y}
and
q_{3}^{X}=f((l_{u},v))=u=q_{3}^{Y}
. And from Definition
22
of the reduced
\sim
-quotient of
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
we have that
q_{2}^{Y}\xrightarrow{i?}q_{3}^{Y}
is a transition in
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}=X
.

*

q^{Y}=(l^{T},v^{T},l^{S},v^{S})\in Q^{Y}
with
v^{S}+d\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
. In this case, it follows from Definitions
19
,
3
, and
22
that
q^{Y}\xrightarrow{d}(l^{T},l^{S},v+d)
in
X
. Furthermore, it follows directly from Definition
19
that
((l^{T},l^{S}),i?,\neg\mathit{Inv}(l^{S}),\emptyset,l_{u})\in E^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
. Since
v^{S}+d\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
, we have
v^{S}+d\models\neg\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
. By definition of
T\backslash\!\backslash S
we have that
\mathit{Inv}(l_{u})=\mathbf{T}
, thus
v+d[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in\emptyset}=v+d\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{u})
. Now, with Definition
3
of semantic we it follows that
(l_{u},v+d)\xrightarrow{i?}(l_{u},v+d)
is a transition in
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
. From the state label renaming function
f
from Lemma
20
we have that
q_{3}^{X}=f((l_{u},v+d))=u=q_{3}^{Y}
. And from Definition
22
of the reduced
\sim
-quotient of
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
we have that
q_{2}^{Y}\xrightarrow{i?}q_{3}^{Y}
is a transition in
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}=X
.

9.

a\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}\cup\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}
,
q_{2}^{Y}=e
,
q_{3}^{Y}=e
. Since the target location is the error location, it holds that
q_{3}\notin P
. Thus this case is not feasible.

Thus, in all feasible cases we can show that
q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{3}
implies
q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}
. Since we have chosen an arbitrarily
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{Y}
, it holds for all
i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{Y}
.

It remains to be shown that
q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i_{\mathit{new}}}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}
and
q_{3}\in P
, since
i_{\mathit{new}}\notin\mathit{Act}_{i}^{Y}
. We only have to consider five cases from Definition
19
that involve
i_{\mathit{new}}
(rule 4, 6, 7, 9, and 10). Using the same arguments as in these cases when we were considering
\Theta^{X}(P)\subseteq\Theta^{Y}(P)
we can conclude that
q_{3}\in P
in all feasible cases for
i_{\mathit{new}}
. Thus the implication also holds for
q_{2}
in
X
.

Thus, in both cases the implication holds. Therefore, we can conclude that
q^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}\Rightarrow q_{2}\in P\wedge\forall i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}:\exists q_{3}\in P:q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}
. As
q_{2}
is chosen arbitrarily, it holds for all
q_{2}\in Q^{X}=Q^{Y}
. Therefore, the left-hand side is true.

•

Assume the right-hand side is true, i.e.,
\exists d^{\prime}\leq d\wedge\exists q_{2},q_{3}\in P\wedge\exists o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{Y}:q\xlongrightarrow{d^{\prime}}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{2}\wedge q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{3}\wedge\forall i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{Y}:\exists q_{4}\in P:q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{4}
. First, focus on the delay. From Definition
19
of the quotient for TIOA it follows that
\mathit{Inv}((l^{T},l^{S}))=\mathbf{T}
. Therefore, with Definition
3
of the semantic and Definition
22
of the
\sim
-reduced quotient of
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
it follows that
q^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}
.

Now, consider the output transition labeled with
o!
. Remember that
\mathit{Act}_{o}^{Y}=\mathit{Act}_{o}^{X}=\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cup\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}
. We have to consider the nine cases from Definition
18
. We can use the exact same argument as before (where now rules 5 and 7 have become infeasible) to show that
q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}
is a transition in
X
for all feasible cases. Since we have chosen an arbitrarily
o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{Y}
, it holds for all
o!\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{Y}
. Therefore, we can conclude that
{q^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{d^{\prime}}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}}\wedge q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}
with
q_{2},q_{3}\in P
.

Finally, consider the input transitions labeled with
i?
. Using the same argument as before, we can show that
q_{2}\xrightarrow{i?}q_{4}
in
Y
is also a transition in
X
, and
q_{4}\in P
. Therefore, we can conclude that
{q^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{d^{\prime}}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}}\wedge q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{o!}{}^{\!\!X}q_{3}\wedge\forall i?\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{X}:\exists q_{4}\in P:q_{2}\xlongrightarrow{i?}{}^{\!\!X}q_{4}
with
q_{2},q_{3},q_{4}\in P
. Thus, the right-hand side is true.

Thus, we have shown that when the left-hand side is true for
q^{Y}
in
Y
, it is also true for
q^{Y}
in
X
; and that when the right-hand side is true for
q^{Y}
in
Y
, it is also true for
q^{Y}
in
X
. Thus,
q^{Y}\in\Theta^{X}(P)
. Since
q^{Y}\in P
was chosen arbitrarily, it holds for all states in
P
. Once we choose
P
to be the fixed-point of
\Theta^{Y}
, we have that
\Theta^{Y}(P)\subseteq\Theta^{X}(P)
.

∎

Finally, we are ready to proof Theorem
11
.

Lemma
11
.

Given specification automata
S=(\mathit{Loc}^{S},l_{0}^{S},\mathit{Act}^{S},\mathit{Clk}^{S},E^{S},\mathit{Inv}^{S})
and
T=(\mathit{Loc}^{T},l_{0}^{T},\allowbreak\mathit{Act}^{T},\mathit{Clk}^{T},E^{T},\allowbreak\mathit{Inv}^{T})
where
\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}=\emptyset
. Then
(\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}})^{\Delta}\simeq(\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}})^{\Delta}
.

Proof of Theorem
11
.

First, observe that the semantic of a TIOA and adversarial pruning do not alter the action set. Therefore, it follows directly that
(\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}})^{\Delta}
and
(\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}})^{\Delta}
have the same action set and partitioning into input and output actions, except that
(\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}})^{\Delta}
has an additional input event
i_{\mathit{new}}
, i.e.,
\mathit{Act}^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\cup\{i_{\mathit{new}}\}=\mathit{Act}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
.

Now, it follows from Lemma
22
that it suffice to show that
(\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho})^{\Delta}\simeq(\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}})^{\Delta}
. It follows from Lemma
20
that there is a bijective function
f
relating states from
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}
and
\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
together. Therefore, we can effectively say that they have the same state set (up to relabeling), i.e.,
Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}}=Q^{\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. For brevity, in the rest of this proof we write we write
X=\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}
,
Y=\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
,
\mathit{Clk}=\mathit{Clk}^{T}\uplus\mathit{Clk}^{S}
, and
v^{S}
and
v^{T}
to indicate the part of a valuation
v
of only the clocks of
S
and
T
, respectively. Note that
x_{\mathit{new}}\notin\mathit{Clk}
, but
x_{\mathit{new}}\in\mathit{Clk}^{X}
.

Let
A=\{q\in Q^{X^{\Delta}}\mid q=((l^{T},l^{S}),v),v\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})\}
. Let
R\subseteq Q^{X^{\Delta}}\times Q^{Y^{\Delta}}
such that
R=\{(q,u)\mid q\in A\}\cup\{(q^{X},q^{Y})\in Q^{X^{\Delta}}\setminus A\times Q^{Y^{\Delta}}\mid q^{X}=q^{Y}\}
. We will show that
R
is a bisimulation relation. First, observe that
(q_{0},q_{0})\in R
. Consider a state pair
(q_{1}^{X},q_{1}^{Y})\in R
. We have to check whether the six cases from Definition
20
of bisimulation hold.

•

q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!X^{\Delta}}q_{2}^{X}
,
q_{2}^{X}\in Q^{X}
, and
a\in\mathit{Act}^{X}\cap\mathit{Act}^{Y}
. Combining Definitions
12
,
18
and
19
it follows that
a\in\mathit{Act}^{S}\cup\mathit{Act}^{T}
. From Definition
12
of adversarial pruning we have that
q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}^{X}
and
q_{1}^{X},q_{2}^{X}\in\mathrm{cons}^{X}
. Following Definition
3
of the semantic and Definition
22
of the reduced
\sim
-quotient of
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
, it follows that there exists an edge
(l_{1},a,\varphi,c,l_{2})\in E^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
with
q_{1}^{X}=(l_{1},v_{1})
,
q_{2}^{X}=(l_{2},v_{2})
,
l_{1},l_{2}\in\mathit{Loc}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
,
v_{1},v_{2}\in[\mathit{Clk}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]
,
v_{1}\models\varphi
,
v_{2}=v_{1}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}
, and
v_{2}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{2})
. Now, consider the ten cases from Definition
19
of quotient of TIOAs. We have to show for feasible each case that we can simulate a transition in
Y
, that the involved states in
Y
are consistent, and that the resulting state pair is again in the bisimulation relation
R
.

1.

a\in\mathit{Act}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}^{T}
,
l_{1}=(l_{1}^{T},l_{1}^{S})
,
l_{2}=(l_{2}^{T},l_{2}^{S})
,
\varphi=\varphi^{T}\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{2}^{T})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{T}}\wedge\varphi^{S}\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{2}^{S})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}}
,
c=c^{T}\cup c^{S}
,
(l_{1}^{T},a,\varphi^{T},c^{T},l_{2}^{T})\in E^{T}
, and
(l_{1}^{S},a,\varphi^{S},c^{S},l_{2}^{S})\in E^{S}
. Since
v_{1}\models\varphi
, it holds that
v_{1}\models\varphi^{T}
,
v_{1}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{2}^{T})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{T}}
,
v_{1}\models\varphi^{S}
,
v_{1}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
, and
v_{1}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{2}^{S})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}}
. Because
\mathit{Clk}^{S}\cap\mathit{Clk}^{T}=\emptyset
, it holds that
v_{1}^{T}\models\varphi^{T}
,
v_{1}^{T}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{2}^{T})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{T}}
,
v_{1}^{S}\models\varphi^{S}
,
v_{1}^{S}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
, and
v_{1}^{S}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{2}^{S})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}}
. Since
v_{2}=v_{1}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}
, it holds that
v_{2}^{T}=v_{1}^{T}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{T}}
and
v_{2}^{S}=v_{1}^{S}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}}
. Therefore,
v_{2}^{T}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{2}^{T})
and
v_{2}^{S}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{2}^{S})
.

Combining all information about
T
, we have that
(l_{1}^{T},a,\varphi^{T},c^{T},l_{2}^{T})\in E^{T}
,
v_{1}^{T}\models\varphi^{T}
,
v_{2}^{T}=v_{1}^{T}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{T}}
, and
v_{2}^{T}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{2}^{T})
. Therefore, from Definition
3
it follows that
(l_{1}^{T},v_{1}^{T})\xlongrightarrow{a}(l_{2}^{T},v_{2}^{T})
in
\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
. Combining all information about
S
, we have that
(l_{1}^{S},a,\varphi^{S},c^{S},l_{2}^{S})\in E^{S}
,
v_{1}^{S}\models\varphi^{S}
,
v_{2}^{S}=v_{1}^{S}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}}
, and
v_{2}^{S}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{2}^{S})
. Therefore, from Definition
3
it follows that
(l_{1}^{S},v_{1}^{S})\xlongrightarrow{a}(l_{2}^{S},v_{2}^{S})
in
\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
.

Now, from Definition
18
it follows that
((l_{1}^{T},v_{1}^{T}),(l_{1}^{S},v_{1}^{S}))=(l_{1}^{T},l_{1}^{S},v_{1})=q_{1}^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!Y}((l_{2}^{T},v_{2}^{T}),\allowbreak(l_{2}^{S},v_{2}^{S}))=(l_{2}^{T},l_{2}^{S},v_{2})=q_{2}^{Y}
in
Y
. Thus, we can simulate a transition in
Y
. Also, observe now that
q_{1}^{X}=q_{1}^{Y}
and
q_{2}^{X}=q_{2}^{Y}
.

2.

a\in\mathit{Act}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}^{T}
,
l_{1}=(l^{T},l_{1}^{S})
,
l_{2}=(l^{T},l_{2}^{S})
,
\varphi=\varphi^{S}\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{2}^{S})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}}
,
c=c^{S}
,
l^{T}\in\mathit{Loc}^{T}
, and
(l_{1}^{S},a,\varphi^{S},c^{S},l_{2}^{S})\in E^{S}
. Since
v_{1}\models\varphi
and
\mathit{Clk}^{S}\cap\mathit{Clk}^{T}=\emptyset
, it holds that
v_{1}^{S}\models\varphi^{S}
,
v_{1}^{S}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
, and
v_{1}^{S}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{2}^{S})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}}
. Since
v_{2}=v_{1}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}
and
c=c^{S}
, it holds that
v_{2}^{S}=v_{1}^{S}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}}
,
v_{2}^{T}=v_{1}^{T}
, and
v_{2}^{S}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{2}^{S})
. Combining all information above about
S
, it follows from Definition
3
that
(l_{1}^{S},v_{1}^{S})\xlongrightarrow{a}(l_{2}^{S},v_{2}^{S})
in
\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
. From Definition
3
it also follows that
(l^{T},v_{1}^{T})\in Q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. Therefore, following Definition
18
it follows that
((l^{T},v_{1}^{T}),(l_{1}^{S},v_{1}^{S}))=(l^{T},l_{1}^{S},v_{1})=q_{1}^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!Y}((l^{T},v_{1}^{T}),(l_{2}^{S},v_{2}^{S}))=(l^{T},l_{2}^{S},v_{2})=q_{2}^{Y}
in
Y
. Thus, we can simulate a transition in
Y
. Also, observe now that
q_{1}^{X}=q_{1}^{Y}
and
q_{2}^{X}=q_{2}^{Y}
.

3.

a\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}
,
l_{1}=(l^{T},l_{1}^{S})
,
l_{2}=l_{u}
,
\varphi=\neg G_{S}
,
c=\emptyset
,
l^{T}\in\mathit{Loc}^{T}
and
G_{S}=\bigvee\{\varphi^{S}\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{2}^{S})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}}\mid(l_{1}^{S},a,\varphi^{S},c^{S},l_{2}^{S})\in E^{S}\}
. Since
v_{1}\models\varphi
and
\mathit{Clk}^{S}\cap\mathit{Clk}^{T}=\emptyset
, it holds that
v_{1}^{S}\models\neg G_{S}
. Therefore,
v_{1}^{S}\not\models G_{S}
, which indicates that
\forall(l_{1}^{S},a,\varphi^{S},c^{S},l_{2}^{S})\in E^{S}
:
v_{1}^{S}\not\models\varphi^{S}\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{2}^{S})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}}
. This means that
v_{1}^{S}\not\models\varphi^{S}
or
v_{1}^{S}\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{2}^{S})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}}
or both, where the second option is equivalent to
v_{1}^{S}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}}\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{2}^{S})
. Following Definition
3
, we can conclude that
(l_{1}^{S},v_{1}^{S})\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{\ a\ }
in
\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
. From Definition
3
it also follows that
(l^{T},v_{1}^{T})\in Q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. Now, following Definition
18
, we have transition
((l^{T},v_{1}^{T}),(l_{1}^{S},v_{1}^{S}))=(l^{T},l_{1}^{S},v_{1})=q_{1}^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!Y}u=q_{2}^{Y}
in
Y
. Thus we can simulate a transition in
Y
. Also, observe now that
q_{1}^{X}=q_{1}^{Y}
and
q_{2}^{X}=q_{2}^{Y}
(where
(l_{u},v_{2})
is mapped into
u
by
f
from Lemma
20
).

4.

a\in\mathit{Act}^{S}\cup\mathit{Act}^{T}
,
l_{1}=(l^{T},l^{S})
,
l_{2}=l_{u}
,
\varphi=\neg\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
,
c=\emptyset
,
l^{T}\in\mathit{Loc}^{T}
, and
l^{S}\in\mathit{Loc}^{S}
. Since
v_{1}\models\varphi
and
\mathit{Clk}^{S}\cap\mathit{Clk}^{T}=\emptyset
, it holds that
v_{1}^{S}\models\neg\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
. Therefore,
v_{1}^{S}\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
. Since
(q_{1}^{X},q_{1}^{Y})\in R
and
v_{1}^{S}\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
, it follows that
q_{1}^{Y}=u
. From Definition
18
it follows that
u=q_{1}^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!Y}u=q_{2}^{Y}
in
Y
. Thus we can simulate a transition in
Y
. Also, observe now that
q_{2}^{X}=q_{2}^{Y}
(where
(l_{u},v_{2})
is mapped into
u
by
f
from Lemma
20
).

5.

a\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}
,
l_{1}=(l_{1}^{T},l_{1}^{S})
,
l_{2}=l_{e}
,
\varphi=\varphi^{S}\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{2}^{S})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}}\wedge\neg G_{T}
,
c=\{x_{\mathit{new}}\}
,
(l_{1}^{S},a,\varphi^{S},c^{S},l_{2}^{S})\in E^{S}
, and
G_{T}=\bigvee\{\varphi^{T}\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{2}^{T})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{T}}\mid(l_{1}^{T},a,\varphi^{T},c^{T},l_{2}^{T})\in E^{T}\}
. Since the target location is the error location, it holds that
q_{2}^{X}\notin\mathrm{cons}^{X}
. Thus this case is not feasible.

6.

a=i_{\mathit{new}}
,
l_{1}=(l^{T},l^{S})
,
l_{2}=l_{e}
,
\varphi=\neg\mathit{Inv}(l^{T})\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
,
c=\{x_{\mathit{new}}\}
,
l^{T}\in\mathit{Loc}^{T}
, and
q^{S}\in\mathit{Loc}^{S}
. This case is infeasible, since
i_{\mathit{new}}\notin\mathit{Act}^{Y}
, thus
i_{\mathit{new}}\notin\mathit{Act}^{X}\cap\mathit{Act}^{Y}
.

7.

a=i_{\mathit{new}}
,
l_{1}=l_{2}=(l_{1}^{T},l_{1}^{S})
,
\varphi=\mathit{Inv}(l^{T})\vee\neg\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
and
c=\emptyset
. This case is infeasible, since
i_{\mathit{new}}\notin\mathit{Act}^{Y}
, thus
i_{\mathit{new}}\notin\mathit{Act}^{X}\cap\mathit{Act}^{Y}
.

8.

a\in\mathit{Act}^{T}\setminus\mathit{Act}^{S}
,
l_{1}=(l_{1}^{T},l^{S})
,
l_{2}=(l_{2}^{T},l^{S})
,
\varphi=\varphi^{T}\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{2}^{T})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{T}}\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
,
c=c^{T}
,
l^{S}\in\mathit{Loc}^{S}
, and
(l_{1}^{T},a,\varphi^{T},c^{T},l_{2}^{T})\in E^{T}
. Since
v_{1}\models\varphi
and
\mathit{Clk}^{S}\cap\mathit{Clk}^{T}=\emptyset
, it holds that
v_{1}^{T}\models\varphi^{T}
and
v_{1}^{T}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{2}^{T})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{T}}
. Since
v_{2}=v_{1}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}
and
c=c^{T}
, it holds that
v_{2}^{T}=v_{1}^{T}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{T}}
,
v_{2}^{S}=v_{1}^{S}
, and
v_{2}^{T}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{2}^{T})
. Combining all information above about
T
, it follows from Definition
3
that
(l_{1}^{T},v_{1}^{T})\xlongrightarrow{a}(l_{2}^{T},v_{2}^{T})
in
\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
. From Definition
3
it also follows that
(l^{S},v_{1}^{S})\in Q^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. Therefore, following Definition
18
it follows that
((l_{1}^{T},v_{1}^{T}),(l^{S},v_{1}^{S}))=(l_{1}^{T},l^{S},v_{1})=q_{1}^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!Y}((l_{2}^{T},v_{2}^{T}),(l^{S},v_{1}^{S}))=(l_{2}^{T},l^{S},v_{2})=q_{2}^{Y}
in
Y
. Thus, we can simulate a transition in
Y
. Also, observe now that
q_{1}^{X}=q_{1}^{Y}
and
q_{2}^{X}=q_{2}^{Y}
.

9.

a\in\mathit{Act}^{S}\cup\mathit{Act}^{T}
,
l_{1}=l_{u}
,
l_{2}=l_{u}
,
\varphi=\mathbf{T}
,
c=\emptyset
. From the construction of the bisimulation relation
R
, we know that if
q_{1}^{X}=f((l_{u},v_{1}))=u
for some valuation
v_{1}
, then
q_{1}^{Y}=u
. From Definition
18
it follows directly that there exists a transition
q_{1}^{Y}=u\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!Y}u=q_{2}^{Y}
in
Y
. Thus, we can simulate a transition in
Y
. Also, observe now that
q_{1}^{X}=q_{1}^{Y}
and
q_{2}^{X}=q_{2}^{Y}
.

10.

a\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{S}\cup\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}
,
l_{1}=l_{e}
,
l_{2}=l_{e}
,
\varphi=x_{\mathit{new}}=0
,
c=\emptyset
. Since the source and target locations are the error location, it holds that
q_{1}^{X},q_{2}^{X}\notin\mathrm{cons}^{X}
. Thus this case is not feasible.

In all feasible cases we can show that
q_{1}^{Y}=q_{1}^{X}
or
q_{1}^{Y}=u
and
q_{2}^{Y}=q_{2}^{X}
. Since
q_{1}^{X},q_{2}^{X}\in\mathrm{cons}^{X}
and
u\in\mathrm{cons}^{Y}
by construction of
u
, it follows from Lemma
25
that
q_{1}^{Y},q_{2}^{Y}\in\mathrm{cons}^{Y}
. Therefore, we can conclude that
q_{1}^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!Y^{\Delta}}q_{2}^{Y}
. And from the construction of the bisimulation relation
R
it follows that
(q_{2}^{X},q_{2}^{Y})\in R
.

•

q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!X^{\Delta}}q_{2}^{X}
,
q_{2}^{X}\in Q^{X}
, and
a=i_{\mathit{new}}
. From Definition
12
of adversarial pruning we have that
q_{1}^{X}\allowbreak{\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!X}}\allowbreak q_{2}^{X}
and
q_{1}^{X},q_{2}^{X}\in\mathrm{cons}^{X}
. Following Definition
3
of the semantic, it follows that there exists an edge
(l_{1},a,\varphi,c,l_{2})\in E^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
with
q_{1}^{X}=(l_{1},v_{1})
,
q_{2}^{X}=(l_{2},v_{2})
,
l_{1},l_{2}\in\mathit{Loc}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
,
v_{1},v_{2}\in[\mathit{Clk}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]
,
v_{1}\models\varphi
,
v_{2}=v_{1}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}
, and
v_{2}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{2})
. There are three cases from Definition
19
of the quotient for TIOA that apply here.

–

l_{1}=(l^{T},l^{S})
,
l_{2}=l_{e}
,
\varphi=\neg\mathit{Inv}(l^{T})\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
,
c=\{x_{\mathit{new}}\}
,
l^{T}\in\mathit{Loc}^{T}
, and
q^{S}\in\mathit{Loc}^{S}
. Since the target location is the error location, it holds that
q_{2}^{X}\notin\mathrm{cons}^{X}
. Thus this case is not feasible.

–

l_{1}=l_{2}=(l_{1}^{T},l_{1}^{S})
,
\varphi=\mathit{Inv}(l^{T})\vee\neg\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
and
c=\emptyset
. Since
c=\emptyset
, it follows that
v_{2}=v_{1}
. Therefore,
q_{1}^{X}=q_{2}^{X}
. Following the second case of Definition
20
and knowing that
(q_{1}^{X},q_{1}^{Y})\in R
, if follows immediately that
(q_{2}^{X},q_{1}^{Y})\in R
. Since
q_{1}^{X}\in\mathrm{cons}^{X}
, it follows from the construction of
R
and Lemma
25
that
q_{1}^{y}=q_{1}^{X}
and thus
q_{1}^{Y}\in\mathrm{cons}^{Y}
.

–

l_{1}=l_{2}
,
l_{2}=l_{e}
,
\varphi=x_{\mathit{new}}
, and
c=\emptyset
. Since the source and target locations are the error location, it holds that
q_{1}^{X},q_{2}^{X}\notin\mathrm{cons}^{X}
. Thus this case is not feasible.

•

q_{1}^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!Y^{\Delta}}q_{2}^{Y}
,
q_{2}^{Y}\in Q^{Y}
, and
a\in\mathit{Act}^{Y}\cap\mathit{Act}^{X}
. Combining Definitions
12
,
18
and
19
it follows that
a\in\mathit{Act}^{S}\cup\mathit{Act}^{T}
. From Definition
12
of adversarial pruning we have that
q_{1}^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{2}^{Y}
and
q_{1}^{Y},q_{2}^{Y}\in\mathrm{cons}^{Y}
. Now, consider the nine cases from Definition
18
of the quotient of TIOTS. We have to show for each feasible case that we can simulate a transition in
X
, that the involved states in
X
are consistent, and that the resulting state pair is again in the bisimulation relation
R
.

1.

a\in\mathit{Act}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}^{T}
,
q_{1}^{Y}=(q_{1}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}},q_{1}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}})
,
q_{2}^{Y}=(q_{2}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}},q_{2}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}})
,
q_{1}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}q_{2}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
, and
q_{1}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\allowbreak{\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}}q_{2}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. From Definition
3
of semantic it follows that there exists an edge
(l_{1}^{T},a,\varphi^{T},c^{T},l_{2}^{T})\in E^{T}
with
q_{1}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l_{1}^{T},v_{1}^{T})
,
q_{2}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l_{2}^{T},v_{2}^{T})
,
l_{1}^{T},l_{2}^{T}\in\mathit{Loc}^{T}
,
v_{1}^{T},v_{2}^{T}\in[\mathit{Clk}^{T}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]
,
v_{1}^{T}\models\varphi^{T}
,
v_{2}^{T}=v_{1}^{T}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{T}}
, and
v_{2}^{T}\models\mathit{Inv}^{T}(l_{2}^{T})
. Similarly, it follows from the same definition that there exists an edge
(l_{1}^{S},a,\varphi^{S},c^{S},l_{2}^{S})\in E^{S}
with
q_{1}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l_{1}^{S},v_{1}^{S})
,
q_{2}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l_{2}^{S},v_{2}^{S})
,
l_{1}^{S},l_{2}^{S}\in\mathit{Loc}^{S}
,
v_{1}^{S},v_{2}^{S}\in[\mathit{Clk}^{S}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]
,
v_{1}^{S}\models\varphi^{S}
,
v_{2}^{S}=v_{1}^{S}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}}
, and
v_{2}^{S}\models\mathit{Inv}^{S}(l_{2}^{S})
. Based on Definition
19
of the quotient for TIOA, we need to consider the following two cases.

–

v_{1}^{S}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
. In this case, there exists an edge
((l_{1}^{T},l_{1}^{S}),a,\varphi^{T}\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{2}^{T})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{T}}\wedge\varphi^{S}\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{2}^{S})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}},c^{T}\cup c^{S},(l_{2}^{T},l_{2}^{S}))
in
T\backslash\!\backslash S
. Let
v_{i},i=1,2
be the valuations that combines the one from
T
with the one from
S
, i.e.
\forall r\in\mathit{Clk}^{T}:v_{i}(r)=v_{i}^{T}(r)
and
\forall r\in\mathit{Clk}^{S}:v_{i}(r)=v_{i}^{S}(r)
. Because
\mathit{Clk}^{T}\cap\mathit{Clk}^{S}=\emptyset
, it holds that
v_{1}\models\varphi^{T}
,
v_{1}\models\varphi^{S}
, and
v_{1}^{S}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
, thus
v_{1}\models\varphi^{T}\wedge\varphi^{S}\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
;
v_{2}=v_{1}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{T}\cup c^{S}}
; and
v_{2}\models\mathit{Inv}^{T}(l_{2}^{T})
and
v_{2}\models\mathit{Inv}^{S}(l_{2}^{S})
, thus
v_{2}\models\mathit{Inv}^{T}(l_{2}^{T})\wedge\mathit{Inv}^{S}(l_{2}^{S})
.

From Definition
3
it now follows that
((l_{1}^{T},l_{1}^{S}),v_{1})\xrightarrow{a}((l_{2}^{T},l_{2}^{S}),v_{2})
is a transition in
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
. Because
\mathit{Clk}^{T}\cap\mathit{Clk}^{S}=\emptyset
, we can rearrange the states into
((l_{1}^{T},l_{1}^{S}),v_{1})=((l_{1}^{T},v_{1}^{S}),(l_{1}^{T},v_{1}^{S}))=q_{1}^{Y}
and
((l_{2}^{T},l_{2}^{S}),v_{2})=((l_{2}^{T},v_{2}^{T}),(l_{2}^{S},v_{2}^{S}))=q_{2}^{Y}
. Thus,
q_{1}^{Y}\xrightarrow{a}q_{2}^{Y}
is a transition in
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}=Y
. Also, observe now that
q_{1}^{X}=q_{1}^{Y}
and
q_{2}^{X}=q_{2}^{Y}
.

–

v_{1}^{S}\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
. From the construction of
R
, it follows that
((l_{1}^{T},l_{1}^{S},v_{1}),u)\in R
, i.e.
q_{1}^{Y}=u
. This contradicts with the start of this case that
q_{2}^{Y}=(q_{2}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}},q_{2}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}})
. Thus this case is infeasible.

2.

a\in\mathit{Act}^{S}\setminus\mathit{Act}^{T}
,
q_{1}^{Y}=(q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}},q_{1}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}})
,
q_{2}^{Y}=(q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}},q_{2}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}})
,
q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\in Q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
, and
q_{1}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}q_{2}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. From Definition
3
of semantic it follows that there exists an edge
(l_{1}^{S},a,\varphi^{S},c^{S},l_{2}^{S})\in E^{S}
with
q_{1}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l_{1}^{S},v_{1}^{S})
,
q_{2}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l_{2}^{S},v_{2}^{S})
,
l_{1}^{S},l_{2}^{S}\in\mathit{Loc}^{S}
,
v_{1}^{S},v_{2}^{S}\in[\mathit{Clk}^{S}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]
,
v_{1}^{S}\models\varphi^{S}
,
v_{2}^{S}=v_{1}^{S}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}}
, and
v_{2}^{S}\models\mathit{Inv}^{S}(l_{2}^{S})
. From the same definition, it follows that
q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l^{T},v^{T})
for some
l^{T}\in\mathit{Loc}^{T}
and
v^{T}\in[\mathit{Clk}^{T}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]
. Based on Definition
19
of the quotient for TIOA, we need to consider the following two cases.

–

v_{1}^{S}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
. In this case, there exists an edge
((l^{T},l_{1}^{S}),a,\varphi^{S}\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{2}^{S})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}},c^{S},\allowbreak(l^{T},l_{2}^{S}))
in
T\backslash\!\backslash S
. Let
v_{i},i=1,2
be the valuations that combines the one from
T
with the one from
S
, i.e.
\forall r\in\mathit{Clk}^{T}:v_{i}(r)=v_{i}^{T}(r)
and
\forall r\in\mathit{Clk}^{S}:v_{i}(r)=v_{i}^{S}(r)
. Because
\mathit{Clk}^{T}\cap\mathit{Clk}^{S}=\emptyset
, it holds that
v_{1}\models\varphi^{S}
, and
v_{1}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
, thus
v_{1}\models\varphi^{S}\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
;
v_{2}=v_{1}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{S}}
; and
v_{2}\models\mathit{Inv}^{S}(l_{2}^{S})
.

Since
\mathit{Inv}((l^{T},l_{2}^{S}))=\mathbf{T}
by definition
T\backslash\!\backslash S
, we have that
v_{2}\models\mathit{Inv}((l^{T},l_{2}^{S}))
. From Definition
3
it now follows that
((l^{T},l_{1}^{S}),v_{1})\xrightarrow{a}((l^{T},l_{2}^{S}),v_{2})
is a transition in
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
. Using Definition
22
of the reduced
\sim
-quotient of
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
and Lemma
20
, we can rearrange the states into
((l^{T},l_{1}^{S}),v_{1})=((l^{T},v_{1}^{T}),(l_{1}^{S},v_{1}^{S}))=q_{1}^{Y}
and
((l^{T},l_{2}^{S}),v_{2})=((l^{T},v_{2}^{T}),(l_{2}^{S},v_{2}^{S}))=q_{2}^{Y}
, and we can show that
q_{1}^{Y}\xrightarrow{a}q_{2}^{Y}
is a transition in
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}=X
. Also, observe now that
q_{1}^{X}=q_{1}^{Y}
and
q_{2}^{X}=q_{2}^{Y}
.

–

v_{1}^{S}\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
. From the construction of
R
, it follows that
((l_{1}^{T},l_{1}^{S},v_{1}),u)\in R
, i.e.
q_{1}^{Y}=u
. This contradicts with the start of this case that
q_{2}^{Y}=(q_{2}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}},q_{2}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}})
. Thus this case is infeasible.

3.

a\in\mathit{Act}^{T}\setminus\mathit{Act}^{S}
,
q_{1}^{Y}=(q_{1}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}},q^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}})
,
q_{2}^{Y}=(q_{2}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}},q^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}})
,
q^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\in Q^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
, and
q_{1}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}q_{2}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
.
From Definition
3
of semantic it follows that there exists an edge
(l_{1}^{T},a,\varphi^{T},c^{T},l_{2}^{T})\in E^{T}
with
q_{1}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l_{1}^{T},v_{1}^{T})
,
q_{2}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l_{2}^{T},v_{2}^{T})
,
l_{1}^{T},l_{2}^{T}\in\mathit{Loc}^{T}
,

,

,

, and

. From the same definition, it follows that
q^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l^{S},v^{S})
for some

and
v^{S}\in[\mathit{Clk}^{S}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]
. Based on Definition

of the quotient for TIOA, we need to consider the following two cases.

–

v_{1}^{S}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
. In this case, there exists an edge
((l_{1}^{T},l^{S}),a,\varphi^{T}\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{2}^{T})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{T}}\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l^{S}),c^{T},\allowbreak(l_{2}^{T},l^{S}))
in
T\backslash\!\backslash S
. Let
v_{i},i=1,2
be the valuations that combines the one from

with the one from
S
, i.e.
\forall r\in\mathit{Clk}^{T}:v_{i}(r)=v_{i}^{T}(r)
and
\forall r\in\mathit{Clk}^{S}:v_{i}(r)=v_{i}^{S}(r)
. Because
\mathit{Clk}^{T}\cap\mathit{Clk}^{S}=\emptyset
, it holds that
v_{1}\models\varphi^{T}
, and
v_{1}\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
, thus
v_{1}\models\varphi^{T}\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
;
v_{2}=v_{1}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c^{T}}

.

Since

. From Definition

((l_{1}^{T},l^{S}),v_{1})\xrightarrow{a}((l_{2}^{T},l^{S}),v_{2})

. Using Definition

of the reduced
\sim
-quotient of
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
and Lemma

, we can rearrange the states into
((l_{1}^{T},l^{S}),v_{1})=((l_{1}^{T},v_{1}^{T}),(l^{S},v_{1}^{S}))=q_{1}^{Y}

, and we can show that

\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}=X
. Also, observe now that

and
q_{2}^{X}=q_{2}^{Y}
.

–

v_{1}^{S}\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
. From the construction of

, it follows that
((l_{1}^{T},l^{S},v_{1}),u)\in R
, i.e.

. This contradicts with the start of this case that
q_{2}^{Y}=(q_{2}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}},q_{2}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}})
. Thus this case is infeasible.

4.

d\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
,
q_{1}^{Y}=(q_{1}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}},q_{1}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}})
,
q_{2}^{Y}=(q_{2}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}},q_{2}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}})
,
q_{1}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}q_{2}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
, and
q_{1}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\allowbreak{\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}}q_{2}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. This case is infeasible, since
a\neq d
(delays will be treated later in the proof).

5.

a\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}
,
q_{1}^{Y}=(q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}},q^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}})
,
q_{2}^{Y}=u
,
q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\in Q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
, and
q^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. From Definition
3
of semantic it follows that
q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l^{T},v^{T})
and
q^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l^{S},v^{S})
. There are two reasons why
q^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
: there might be no edge in
E^{S}
labeled with action
a
from location
l^{S}
or none of the edges labeled with
a
from
l^{S}
are enabled. An edge
(l^{S},a,\varphi,c,l^{S\prime})\in E^{S}
is not enabled if
v^{S}\not\models\varphi
or
v^{S}[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{S\prime})
(or both), which can also be written as
v^{S}\not\models\varphi\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l^{S\prime})[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in c}
. Looking at the third rule in Definition
19
of the quotient for TIOA, we have that
((l^{T},l^{S}),a,\neg G_{S},\emptyset,l_{u})\in E^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
and
v^{S}\not\models G_{S}
, or
v^{S}\models\neg G_{S}
. Because
\mathit{Clk}^{T}\cap\mathit{Clk}^{S}=\emptyset
, it holds that
v\models\neg G_{S}
.

Now, since
\mathit{Inv}(l_{u})=\textbf{T}
and no clocks are reset, it holds that
v[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in\emptyset}=v\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{u})
. From Definition
3
it now follows that
((l^{T},l^{S}),v)\xrightarrow{a}(l_{u},v_{2})
is a transition in
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
. From the state label renaming function
f
from Lemma
20
we have that
q_{2}^{X}=f((l_{u},v_{2}))=u=q_{2}^{Y}
and
q_{1}^{X}=q_{1}^{Y}
. And from Definition
22
of the reduced
\sim
-quotient of
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
we have that
q_{1}^{Y}\xrightarrow{a}q_{2}^{Y}
is a transition in
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}=X
.

6.

d\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
,
q_{1}^{Y}=(q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}},q^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}})
,
q_{2}^{Y}=u
,
q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\in Q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
, and
q^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. This case is infeasible, since
a\neq d
(delays will be treated later in the proof).

7.

a\in\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}\cap\mathit{Act}_{o}^{T}
,
q_{1}^{Y}=(q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}},q^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}})
,
q_{2}^{Y}=e
,
q^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
, and
q^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. Since the target state is the error state, it holds that
q_{2}^{Y}\notin\mathrm{cons}^{Y}
. Thus this case is not feasible.

8.

a\in\mathit{Act}^{T}\cup\mathit{Act}^{S}\cup\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
,
q_{1}^{Y}=u
,
q_{2}^{Y}=u
. From the construction of
R
it follows that there are two options for
q_{1}^{X}
for the pair
(q_{1}^{X},u)\in R
.

–

q_{1}^{X}=u\ (=(l_{u},v))
. In this case, it follows directly from Definition
19
that
(l_{u},a,\mathbf{T},\emptyset,l_{u})\in E^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
. Since any valuation satisfies a true guard and by definition of
T\backslash\!\backslash S
that
\mathit{Inv}(l_{u})=\mathbf{T}
, we have with Definition
3
of semantic that
(l_{u},v)\xrightarrow{a}(l_{u},v)
is a transition in
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
. From the state label renaming function
f
from Lemma
20
we have that
q_{1}^{X}=q_{1}^{Y}
and
q_{2}^{X}=f((l_{u},v))=u=q_{2}^{Y}
. And from Definition
22
of the reduced
\sim
-quotient of
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
we have that
q_{1}^{Y}\xrightarrow{a}q_{2}^{Y}
is a transition in
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}=X
.

–

q_{1}^{X}=((l^{T},l^{S}),v)\in Q^{X^{\Delta}}
with
v\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
. In this case, it follows directly from Definition
19
that
((l^{T},l^{S}),a,\neg\mathit{Inv}(l^{S}),\emptyset,l_{u})\in E^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
. Since
v\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
, we have
v\models\neg\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
. By definition of
T\backslash\!\backslash S
we have that
\mathit{Inv}(l_{u})=\mathbf{T}
, thus
v[r\mapsto 0]_{r\in\emptyset}=v\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{u})
. Now, with Definition
3
of semantic we it follows that
(l_{u},v)\xrightarrow{a}(l_{u},v)
is a transition in
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
. From the state label renaming function
f
from Lemma
20
we have that
q_{2}^{X}=f((l_{u},v))=u=q_{2}^{Y}
. And from Definition
22
of the reduced
\sim
-quotient of
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
we have that
q_{1}^{Y}\xrightarrow{a}q_{2}^{Y}
is a transition in
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}^{\rho}=X
.

9.

a\in\mathit{Act}_{i}^{T}\cup\mathit{Act}_{o}^{S}
,
q_{1}^{Y}=e
,
q_{2}^{Y}=e
. Since the source and target states are the error state, it holds that
q_{1}^{Y},q_{2}^{Y}\notin\mathrm{cons}^{Y}
. Thus this case is not feasible.

In all feasible cases we can show that
q_{1}^{X}=q_{1}^{Y}
or
q_{1}^{X}=((l^{T},l^{S}),v)
with
v\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l^{S})
and
q_{2}^{X}=q_{2}^{Y}
. Since
q_{1}^{Y},q_{2}^{Y}\in\mathrm{cons}^{Y}
and
((l^{T},l^{S}),v)\in Q^{X^{\Delta}}
by construction of
R
, it follows from Lemma
25
that
q_{1}^{X},q_{2}^{X}\in\mathrm{cons}^{X}
. Therefore, we can conclude that
q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!X^{\Delta}}q_{2}^{X}
. And from the construction of the bisimulation relation
R
it follows that
(q_{2}^{X},q_{2}^{Y})\in R
.

•

q_{1}^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{a}{}^{\!\!Y^{\Delta}}q_{2}^{Y}
,
q_{2}^{Y}\in Q^{Y}
, and
a\in\mathit{Act}^{Y}\setminus\mathit{Act}^{X}
. This case is infeasible, as
\mathit{Act}^{X}=\mathit{Act}^{Y}\cup\{i_{\mathit{new}}\}
.

•

q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X^{\Delta}}q_{2}^{X}
,
q_{2}^{X}\in Q^{X}
, and
d\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
. From Definition
12
of adversarial pruning we have that
q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X}q_{2}^{X}
and
q_{1}^{X},q_{2}^{X}\in\mathrm{cons}^{X}
. Following Definition
3
of the semantic and Definition
22
of the reduced
\sim
-quotient of
\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
, it follows that
q_{1}^{X}=(l_{1},v_{1})
and
q_{2}^{X}=(l_{1},v_{1}+d)
with
l_{1}\in\mathit{Loc}^{T\backslash\!\backslash S}
,
v_{1}\in[\mathit{Clk}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]
,
v_{1}+d\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1})
, and
\forall d^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},d^{\prime}<d:v_{1}+d^{\prime}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1})
. Since
q_{1}^{X}\in\mathrm{cons}^{X}
, it follows that
l_{1}=(l_{1}^{T},l_{1}^{S})
or
l_{1}=l_{u}
. Therefore, from Definition
19
of the quotient for TIOA, we have that and
\mathit{Inv}(l_{1})=\mathbf{T}
. Note that we do not directly get information about whether the valuation
v_{1}+d
satisfy the location invariant in
T
or
S
.

Now consider first the simple case where
l_{1}=l_{u}
. From Definition
18
of the quotient for TIOTS, it follows directly that
u\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!Y}u
. And note with Lemma
20
that
q_{2}^{X}=f((l_{u},v_{1}+d))=u=q_{2}^{Y}
and thus
(q_{2}^{X},q_{2}^{Y})\in R
.

Now consider the case where
l_{1}=(l_{1}^{T},l_{1}^{S})
. We have to consider whether delays are possible in
\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
and
\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
in order to show that
Y
can follow the delay and that the resulting state pair is in the bisimulation relation
R
.

–

q_{1}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}q_{2}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
and
q_{1}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}q_{2}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. In this case, it follows from Definition
3
of the semantic that
q_{1}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l_{1}^{T},v_{1}^{T})
,
\forall c\in\mathit{Clk}^{T}:v_{1}^{T}(c)=v_{1}(c)
,
q_{2}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l_{1}^{T},v_{1}^{T}+d)
,
v_{1}^{T}+d\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{T})
, and
\forall d^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},d^{\prime}<d:v_{1}^{T}+d^{\prime}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{T})
; similarly we have that
q_{1}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l_{1}^{S},v_{1}^{S})
,
\forall c\in\mathit{Clk}^{S}:v_{1}^{S}(c)=v_{1}(c)
,
q_{2}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l_{1}^{S},v_{1}^{S}+d)
,
v_{1}^{S}+d\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
, and
\forall d^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},d^{\prime}<d:v_{1}^{S}+d^{\prime}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
. From Definition
18
of the quotient for TIOTS it follows that
(q_{1}^{T},q_{1}^{S})\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!Y}(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{S})
. Observe with Lemma
20
that
q_{1}^{Y}=(q_{1}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}},q_{1}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}})=(l_{1}^{T},l_{1}^{S},v_{1})=q_{1}^{X}
and
q_{2}^{Y}=(q_{2}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}},q_{2}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}})=(l_{1}^{T},l_{1}^{S},v_{2})=q_{2}^{X}
. Thus
(q_{2}^{X},q_{2}^{Y})\in R
.

–

q_{1}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}q_{2}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
and
q_{1}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. In this case, it follows from Definition
3
of the semantic that
q_{1}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l_{1}^{T},v_{1}^{T})
,
\forall c\in\mathit{Clk}^{T}:v_{1}^{T}(c)=v_{1}(c)
,
q_{2}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l_{1}^{T},v_{1}^{T}+d)
,
v_{1}^{T}+d\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{T})
, and
\forall d^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},d^{\prime}<d:v_{1}^{T}+d^{\prime}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{T})
; similarly we have that
q_{1}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l_{1}^{S},v_{1}^{S})
,
\forall c\in\mathit{Clk}^{S}:v_{1}^{S}(c)=v_{1}(c)
, and
\exists d^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},d^{\prime}\leq d:v_{1}^{S}+d^{\prime}\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
. We have to consider two cases.

*

v_{1}^{S}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
. Since
\mathit{Clk}^{T}\cap\mathit{Clk}^{S}=\emptyset
,
v_{1}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
. Since
(q_{1}^{X},q_{1}^{Y})\in R
and
v_{1}^{S}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
, we have that
q_{1}^{Y}=q_{1}^{X}
. From Definition
18
of the quotient for TIOTS, it follows that
q_{1}^{Y}=((l_{1}^{T},v_{1}^{T}),(l_{1}^{S},v_{1}^{S}))\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!Y}u=q_{2}^{Y}
. From the construction of
R
we have that
q_{2}^{X}\in A
, thus we can confirm that
(q_{2}^{T},q_{2}^{Y})\in R
.

*

v_{1}^{S}\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
. Again, since
\mathit{Clk}^{T}\cap\mathit{Clk}^{S}=\emptyset
,
v_{1}\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
. Since
(q_{1}^{X},q_{1}^{Y})\in R
and
v_{1}^{S}\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
, we have that
q_{1}^{X}\in A
, thus
q_{1}^{Y}=u
. From Definition
18
of the quotient for TIOTS, it follows that
u\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!Y}u
. And by construction of
R
it follows that
(q_{2}^{X},q_{2}^{Y})\in R
.

–

q_{1}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}q_{2}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
and
q_{1}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. This case follows the exact same reasoning as the one above, since Definition
18
of the quotient for TIOTS does not care whether a delay
d
is possible in
\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
once it is not possible in
\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}
.

–

q_{1}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}q_{2}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
and
q_{1}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}q_{2}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. In this case, it follows directly from Definition
18
of the quotient for TIOTS that there is no delay possible in
Y
, i.e.,
(q_{1}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}},q_{1}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}})\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}\backslash\!\backslash\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. It follows from Definition
3
of the semantic that
q_{1}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l_{1}^{T},v_{1}^{T})
,
\forall c\in\mathit{Clk}^{T}:v_{1}^{T}(c)=v_{1}(c)
, and
\exists d^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},d^{\prime}\leq d:v_{1}^{T}+d^{\prime}\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{T})
; similarly we have that
q_{1}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l_{1}^{S},v_{1}^{S})
,
\forall c\in\mathit{Clk}^{S}:v_{1}^{S}(c)=v_{1}(c)
,
q_{2}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l_{1}^{S},v_{1}^{S}+d)
,
v_{1}^{S}+d\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
, and
\forall d^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},d^{\prime}<d:v_{1}^{S}+d^{\prime}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
. Without loss of generality, we can assume that
v_{1}^{T}+0\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{T})
18
18

18

In case there would be a
d^{\prime}<d
such that
v_{1}^{T}+d^{\prime}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{T})
, we can use the first case to simulate the delay
d^{\prime}
in
Y
.
, which simplifies to
v_{1}^{T}\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{T})
. Combining this information, we have that
v_{1}\models\neg\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{T})\wedge\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
, where we used the fact that
\mathit{Clk}^{T}\cap\mathit{Clk}^{S}=\emptyset
. Now, using Definition
19
of the quotient for TIOA and Definition
3
of the semantics, we have that
(l_{1}^{T},l_{1}^{S},v_{1})\xlongrightarrow{i_{\mathit{new}}}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\backslash\!\backslash S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}(l_{e},v_{1})
. Since
(l_{e},v_{1})\notin\mathrm{cons}^{X}
and
i_{\mathit{new}}
is an input, it follows that
(l_{1}^{T},l_{1}^{S},v_{1})=q_{1}^{X}\notin\mathrm{cons}^{X}
. This contradicts with our assumption that
q_{1}^{X}\in\mathrm{cons}^{X}
. Therefore, this case is infeasible.

In all feasible cases we can show that
(q_{2}^{X},q_{2}^{Y})\in R
. Since
q_{1}^{X},q_{2}^{X}\in\mathrm{cons}^{X}
and
A\subseteq Q^{X^{\Delta}}
by construction of
R
, it follows from Lemma
25
that
q_{1}^{Y},q_{2}^{Y}\in\mathrm{cons}^{Y}
. Therefore, we can conclude that
q_{1}^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!Y^{\Delta}}q_{2}^{Y}
.

•

q_{1}^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!Y^{\Delta}}q_{2}^{Y}
,
q_{2}^{Y}\in Q^{Y}
, and
d\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
. From Definition
12
of adversarial pruning we have that
q_{1}^{Y}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!Y}q_{2}^{Y}
and
q_{1}^{Y},q_{2}^{Y}\in\mathrm{cons}^{Y}
. Consider the following three cases from Definition
18
of the quotient for TIOTS.

–

q_{1}^{Y}=(q_{1}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}},q_{1}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}})
,
q_{2}^{Y}=(q_{2}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}},q_{2}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}})
,
q_{1}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}q_{2}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
, and
q_{1}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}q_{2}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. From Definition
3
of the semantic it follows that
q_{1}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l_{1}^{T},v_{1}^{T})
,
q_{2}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l_{1}^{T},v_{1}^{T}+d)
,
v_{1}^{T}+d\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{T})
,
\forall d^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},d^{\prime}<d:v_{1}^{T}+d^{\prime}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{T})
,
q_{1}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l_{1}^{S},v_{1}^{S})
,
q_{2}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l_{1}^{S},v_{1}^{S}+d)
,
v_{1}^{S}+d\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
, and
\forall d^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},d^{\prime}<d:v_{1}^{S}+d^{\prime}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
. Now, from Definition
19
of the quotient for TIOA we have that
\mathit{Inv}((l_{1}^{S},l_{1}^{T}))=\mathbf{T}
in
T\backslash\!\backslash S
, thus using Definitions
3
and
22
we have
q_{1}^{X}=(l_{1}^{S},l_{1}^{T},v_{1})\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X}(l_{1}^{S},l_{1}^{T},v_{1}+d)=q_{2}^{X}
. Observe that
q_{1}^{X}=q_{1}^{Y}
and
q_{2}^{X}=q_{2}^{Y}
, thus
q_{2}^{X},q_{2}^{Y}\in R
.

–

q_{1}^{Y}=(q_{1}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}},q_{1}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}})
,
q_{2}^{Y}=u
, and
q_{1}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}\arrownot\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}q_{2}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}
. From Definition
3
of the semantic it follows that
q_{1}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l_{1}^{T},v_{1}^{T})
,
q_{1}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}}=(l_{1}^{S},v_{1}^{S})
, and
\exists d^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0},d^{\prime}\leq d:v_{1}^{S}+d^{\prime}\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
. Now, from Definition
19
of the quotient for TIOA we have that
\mathit{Inv}((l_{1}^{S},l_{1}^{T}))=\mathbf{T}
in
T\backslash\!\backslash S
, thus using Definitions
3
and
22
we have
q_{1}^{X}=(l_{1}^{S},l_{1}^{T},v_{1})\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X}(l_{1}^{S},l_{1}^{T},v_{1}+d)=q_{2}^{X}
. We have to consider two cases to show that
(q_{2}^{X},q_{2}^{Y})\in R
.

*

v_{1}^{S}\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
. In this case
q_{1}^{X}\notin A
and
q_{2}^{X}\in A
. Therefore,
(q_{2}^{X},q_{2}^{Y})\in R
.

*

v_{1}^{S}\not\models\mathit{Inv}(l_{1}^{S})
. In this case
q_{1}^{X},q_{2}^{X}\in A
. From the construction of
R
it follows that any state from
A
can only be related to state
u
in
Y
, but
q_{1}^{Y}=(q_{1}^{\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}},q_{1}^{\llbracket S\rrbracket_{\mathrm{sem}}})
. This contradiction renders this case infeasible.

–

q_{1}^{Y}=u
and
q_{2}^{Y}=u
. From Definition
19
of the quotient for TIOA, it follows directly that
(l_{u},v)\ {\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X}}\allowbreak(l_{u},v)
for any
v\in[\mathit{Clk}\mapsto\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}]
. And note with Lemma
20
that
q_{1}^{X}=q_{2}^{X}=f((l_{u},v))=u=q_{1}^{Y}=q_{2}^{Y}
and thus
(q_{2}^{X},q_{2}^{Y})\in R
.

In all feasible cases we can show that
(q_{2}^{X},q_{2}^{Y})\in R
. Since
q_{1}^{Y},q_{2}^{Y}\in\mathrm{cons}^{Y}
and
A\subseteq Q^{X^{\Delta}}
by construction of
R
, it follows from Lemma
25
that
q_{1}^{X},q_{2}^{X}\in\mathrm{cons}^{X}
. Therefore, we can conclude that
q_{1}^{X}\xlongrightarrow{d}{}^{\!\!X^{\Delta}}q_{2}^{X}
.

We have show for state pair
(q_{1}^{X},q_{1}^{Y})\in R
that all the six cases of bisimulation hold. Since we have chosen an arbitrary state pair from
R
, it holds for all state pairs in
R
. This concludes the proof.

∎
